**The settled policy** (owner, 2026-11-22): every calendar is read through authenticated, private, read-only access. **What Proton offers** (support pages read 2026-11-23): "Proton Calendar doesn't support CalDAV, so you can't set up a direct two-way sync with an external calendar. The only option right now is to subscribe to a calendar via a shared link"; "Anyone with the shared calendar link can view the calendar", and a full-view link "contains the key required to decrypt the calendar"; sharing with Proton users works only for Proton accounts in Proton's own apps (the invitee accepts and receives the calendar key); a calendar can be exported as an ICS file by hand; Bridge serves mail only. No 2025–2026 announcement of CalDAV, an API or Bridge calendar support was found.

**What is missing, exactly:** an interface through which a non-Proton client, authenticated as an account the calendar is shared with, can read that calendar's events and changes without anyone else being able to read them — such as CalDAV with account authentication, a documented authenticated API, or calendar support in Bridge.

**Consequences:**
- The Proton Calendar row is `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (owner and Claude; provider interface missing). The required-integrations gate and the R2 release gate stay open.
- Nothing else can close the row: not a bearer-readable link, even for a disposable calendar; not a manual export; not a fixture; and not a working Bridge mail connection.
- **This is not a pending owner decision:** the policy is settled. It is listed in §22 as an unmet release condition with its cause.
- **Retest path:** `docs/providers/PROTON-CALENDAR.md` names the sources to re-read (the subscribe-to-external-calendar FAQ, Bridge's release notes, Proton's support pages and announcements). They are re-read before every release candidate and at least every 90 days, and each check is recorded with its date and finding in the provider-evidence log. If Proton adds a supported authenticated interface, a design for it — with any permission it needs — comes back as a plan revision, and the six cases below run against it.

**The six future cases, defined now and blocked until an interface exists** (`OV-CAL-PROTON-NN`; each `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT`, owner and Claude, provider interface missing; executor once runnable: Claude for the fixture part against a controlled server of the new interface, the owner for the live part):

| NN | Capability | Required evidence when runnable |
|---|---|---|
| 01 | Authenticated, private access: the calendar shared with the dedicated Proton account found through the interface, under that account's own authentication; no link anyone holding it could open | The discovery record; the share's read-only role as the interface reports it and as the owner set it in Proton's app |
| 02 | Read-only at the provider: no write method implemented, no write scope or role requested; the calendar unchanged | The request log; the owner's check that the calendar is unchanged |
| 03 | Initial sync of the window: zones and DST, recurrence with moved and cancelled instances, all-day and multi-day events by R1's rules (§8.4.2) | Every owner-seeded event once, at its correct interval |
| 04 | Incremental sync of an edit and a cancellation in Proton's app, within the stated propagation bound | Each change applied once within the bound |
| 05 | Revocation: the share removed → `needs_authorization`; the days shown unverified, never free | The connector state and the planner's view after the owner removes the share |
| 06 | Restart mid-sync, and expiry of whatever incremental state the interface uses → a full read staged, no duplicate events, no false free time | Kill injected → the owner restarts Core through `host-step`; the staged resync published once |

**Acceptance (M7, M8; S-21, S-22, S-23; owner live sessions LS-1–LS-3):** the cases per provider, capability and environment are listed in §10.9.

## 9. Milestones (R2-DEV-01)

Every milestone ends with:
- Fedora debug and release builds and `swift format lint`;
- temporary verification in a fresh run directory;
- scenario records;
- the owner-harness parts the milestone owes (below), built from a clean checkout and run in fixture mode;
- file-by-file commits ending at a buildable checkpoint (§13);
- a checkpoint update and a short report.

**Dependencies on owner sessions:**
- Owner sessions follow the milestone shown. Fedora work never waits for them, except where a session result is a stated prerequisite: **N-1 gates the production nearby transport (M6): a design is written only after its own proof passed at X1, and if both proofs fail a redesign comes back as a plan revision; E-1 gates the production retrieval design (M7): a failing platform gets a redesign whose proof must pass there before release.** Both are release requirements (§22).
- Components are introduced only when their module exists. M5 apps don't use fallback inference (M7) or notification ownership (M8) until those milestones add them.

**Container use:**
- **M2:** the first real use of the rootless engine, running the tmpfs exhaustion harness. It records the engine, context, architecture, image digests and results.
- **M3:** commit the host-command dispatcher and its verification Compose configuration for X2, with a buildable development image running the M3 Core in the authorized rootless engine. Verify from a clean M3 checkout before X2.
- **M4:** the first production image; its digest goes into the external build record.

**Firing identity across milestones.** The restore checks at M3 and M4 (S-11, N-A) need occurrence and firing identity, slot holds and reservations before the device side exists. `LisaFiringIdentity` and the Core side of ownership are therefore built at M3 and driven by a scripted device client. M8 adds the device side and repeats those scenarios with the real implementation.

**The owner-verification harness is delivered with the milestones** (§10.8). Claude builds each part; the owner runs its device and live cases. Every Apple runner and live-session guide ships at or before the milestone that precedes its session.

| Milestone | Buildable afterward | Proposed evidence | Owner-harness delivery (§10.8) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M0 — Baseline** | The whole R1 package on Fedora; frozen R1 sources, binaries, client harness and fixtures (§8.18); `R2-CHECKPOINT.md` | The unmodified R1 suite in debug and release, outcomes recorded as they are; failures classified (§10.3); narrow production fixes, one per commit, then a rerun; `lisa demo`; hashes. R2-L01 (first), R2-L02 (baseline) | — |
| **M1 — Contracts and Apple proofs** | LisaSyncProtocol, LisaTrust, LisaCrypto additions, LisaArchive codec; `Apps/Proof` (syntax-checked) | Codec, size-derivation and token vectors; Merkle paging vectors; the proof-only certificate encoder checked with `openssl`. R2-L05 (codec), L16 (signatures, rollback), L32 (encoding). **X1:** N-1 (both designs' proofs on nine cells), E-1 (literals and redirects included), E-2 | **Harness core:** `lisa-verify` with fixture, device and live modes, the result schema, reports and redacted export, case and run IDs, **the action ledger with the counting controlled servers**, resume and cleanup, the Apple preflight reading the signed products' entitlements and profiles, with identifiers derived from the owner's builds; quick start and Apple setup guide. **X1 runner** (`Apps/OwnerVerification`, test plan `X1`, `pair-session`). S-24 (fixture mode, the four interruption variants) |

S-06 (with the search cases around a local edit and a rejection), S-07 (replica part), S-08 (replica part: deferral, the overlay watermark and constant-size publication, absence in both orders with what a replica shows from an older proof, zone-unverified floating rows and all-day rows read in the planning zone), S-17. Peak staging, index and WAL sizes | — |

Instrumented SQLite checks on Core (S-17 Core part). R2-L04 (direct), L05, L06 (Core), L08 (Core), L13, L14, L16, L28, L29, L33 (Core), L34; L02 again. ## 10. Verification

### 10.1 Evidence classes, reported separately

| Class | Covers | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Fedora host | Builds, the R1 suite, R2 harnesses, the demo, host performance | This account; Fedora 45, x86_64, Swift 6.3.2 |
| Rootless container | Container harnesses (from M2), image builds and runs (from M4), controlled servers | The owner's rootless engine on this host |
| Target host | VPN and off-VPN reachability over IPv4 and IPv6, SELinux confinement, cutover | The owner's server, run by the owner |
| arm64 | The arm64 image | R1's historical run only: 376 debug and 376 release passes on Debian 13/aarch64 with Swift 6.4.2, in R1's retired environment (§1.4). R2 build and run `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (Claude; no arm64 environment) until evidenced; a genuine cross-build would be recorded separately |
| Apple hardware | Builds and device checks | Owner sessions X1–X7, run by the owner with the owner harness |
| Simulated | In-memory peer link, simulated notification center, scripted providers, controlled provider-shaped servers | Labeled; never counted as another class |
| Controlled real-protocol server | A real server implementation run locally with no owner account: the controlled local Ollama container, and the official Proton Mail Bridge in its P-09a sidecar with no account (`OV-MAIL-PROTON-17`) | Labeled; reported apart from the owner's own deployment |
| Owner live | Real provider accounts, real messages, notifications, model charges and calls, under the owner's identity | Owner live sessions LS-1–LS-3 and the live parts of X5 and X6; results the owner chooses to share, redacted |

**Statuses, executors and causes.** Every check carries one of R2's statuses, its **executor** (Claude or the owner) and its actual **cause**:
- `PASS` or `FAIL`, with the run's evidence location;
- `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` — a missing platform, hardware or software (named), an environment configuration, a provider capability that doesn't exist, or a pending physical or owner session;
- `NOT_RUN_CREDENTIALS` — an otherwise authorized run without the credentials or account access it needs. For an owner-run check, this means the owner hasn't yet provisioned the dedicated account, grant, key or profile it names;
- `BLOCKED_PERMISSION` — a required action by that executor lacks authorization: for example, any implementation, the P-09a Bridge sidecar included, before the owner approves this plan;
- `OUT_OF_SCOPE`.

Claude's lack of access to the owner's accounts is a standing boundary, not a pending item. No check requiring an owner account is ever assigned to Claude; each such check is the owner's and waits on the owner's checklist, with its own cause, until the owner runs it. Only a completed owner-run check supplies a live or physical result. Failed and unverified required rows, and their gates, stay open.

**Result levels for integrations and devices,** never merged:

| Result | Meaning | Executor |
|---|---|---|
| `harness-ready` | The case exists in the owner harness, builds from a clean checkout, and its fixture mode passes | Claude |
| `fixture-pass` | The production adapter passes against deterministic fixtures and controlled servers | Claude |
| `provider-accepted` | A real provider accepted the request in an owner-run case (for example SMTP 250, Graph 202, APNs 200, Twilio 201) | Owner |
| `owner-verified` | The owner observed the required outcome end to end: the email in the mailbox, the alert on the device, the call and its spoken message, the calendar state read correctly | Owner |

A required live row closes only at `owner-verified`, with every fixture and fault case of that row at `fixture-pass` (§10.9).

`docs/REQUIREMENTS-MATRIX.md` keeps an **Unavailable** list with each blocked check's executor and exact cause, and independent work continues.

### 10.2 Temporary harnesses and scenario records (O-6)

- Each run gets a fresh `.tmp/r2/<run-id>/`. It holds a separate SwiftPM package that depends on the root package by path, uses swift-testing, and calls public APIs only. Nothing in it is committed, overwritten or removed.
- Frozen R1 artifacts come from M0 (§8.18).
- `docs/VERIFICATION-SCENARIOS.md` is tracked. For every scenario it records commands, inputs, versions, hashes, expected outcomes and the evidence location of each run.
- The tracked owner-verification harness (§10.8) is a separate deliverable and never uses these temporary harnesses or their files.

### 10.3 The R1 suite (O-5, P-07)

- **At M0** the original suite runs unchanged, first in debug and then in release, and its outcomes are recorded as they are. Narrow production fixes follow, one per commit, then a rerun. Later runs: M3, M5, M8, M9.
- **Each failure is classified** as an implementation regression (fixed in the implementation), an explicitly replaced requirement (reported as "replaced by <R2 ID>", test unchanged), or an environment-specific issue (cause and evidence reported).
- **Changing a test or test-support file** needs a request naming the file, the reason and the evidence. The original outcome stays in the record regardless.
- **Source compatibility kept for the suite:**
  - LisaHost's `application`, `close`, `deviceTransport`, `runtime`, `recorder`, `ownerTransport`, `storage`, `recoveryNotes`, `send`, `router`, `importDirectory`, `registerLocalInstallation`, `ownerSettings`, `logger` and `backup`;
  - the facade's commands;
  - LisaClient's typed client, `OfflineQueue`, `URLSessionTransport`, `HostEndpoint` and `NotificationActionHandler`.

  The local-notification installation stays as an optional host capability, because 17 test files use it.

### 10.4 Controlled substitutes (test-only, labeled)

- **From R1:** the scripted HTTP transport; loopback TLS servers with run-time certificates; scripted IMAP, SMTP and APNs servers; synthetic keys and devices.
- **New for R2, in the temporary harnesses:**
  - the in-memory peer link;
  - loopback OpenAI-, Anthropic- and Ollama-shaped servers;
  - the libcurl pinning transport;
  - a response-dropping relay, and a relay that captures the exact bytes it forwards (S-03);
  - the test resolver and byte stream for destinations, including a resolver that synthesizes AAAA answers for chosen names only;
  - the simulated notification center, with an injectable crash between the OS add and the recorded success, and an adjustable wall clock;
  - a scripted device client for M3 assignments, reports and local scheduling (replaced by the real device side at M8);
  - a harness network that partitions one Core host from another while a client still reaches it, and lets a scheduler and a local command run on the cut-off host (S-10);
  - scripted Twilio and SMTP accept-after-cut servers, and a journal-writer that leaves a claim open at the cut (S-11);
  - a clock offset for the frozen R1 host, to run past its 30-day ledger retention, and a read-only reader of R1's database (S-02);
  - a trust-bundle builder that produces valid, tampered, foreign-dataset and incomplete bundles (S-10);
  - a partition-repair driver that delays snapshots and injects pages during a repair, and local edits and rejections at chosen points around publication (S-06, S-08);
  - a second, uninventoried restore and a device that reports its head only after promotion (S-10); an unanchored backup carrying an extra active roster entry (S-10);
  - an OAuth provider whose access tokens stay usable for a set time after a grant revocation, Gmail-shaped Sent lookups by `Message-ID`, and a reminder clock that misses its due time before a backup (S-11);
  - a legacy-decision driver that kills the CLI after Core's commit and restarts Core (S-03);
  - a frozen-R1 maintenance target: a copy of an R1 data directory with R1 stopped or running, to check the lock and the maintenance transactions against R1's own services (S-02);
  - a planning-zone switcher with floating, all-day, zoned and UTC events across DST days (S-08);
  - controlled Graph, Gmail API, Calendar API and CalDAV servers (with label semantics, history expiry, sync-token expiry and 410, shared-calendar properties), OAuth servers that can issue a token for the wrong account, IMAP servers that can reset `UIDVALIDITY` and serve identical messages without `Message-ID`, IMAP and SMTP servers with a self-signed certificate (as Bridge's) and with a different one, and a Twilio-shaped server with call-status progression and a call log (S-21, S-23);
  - **the controlled local Ollama server:** the `ollama/ollama` image, pinned by digest, with a small public model pulled into it, run in the rootless engine with no account. It exercises Ollama's real protocol and is reported as a controlled real-protocol server, never as the owner's deployment (S-22);
  - container `tmpfs` exhaustion and `max_page_count`;
  - a pinned-reader driver;
  - a wall-clock offset for clock-step tests;
  - run-time CAs and intermediates;
  - frozen R1 binaries, client and fixtures;
  - synthetic Mac, iPhone and iPad replicas.
- **Limits:** none contacts a live service, Apple, a model provider or the owner's network. The owner-verification harness carries its own controlled servers and fixtures, written separately (§10.8).

### 10.5 Deciding experiments

| ID | Question | Experiment and success criterion | Who and when | Gates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| N-1 | Nearby transport security | Both designs on Mac–iPhone, Mac–iPad and iPhone–iPad, on shared Wi-Fi, without an access point and without Internet. Success means: correct peer identification; refusal of an unenrolled identity; an exporter-bound hello; refusal of a wrong binding; reconnection after kill and Wi-Fi toggle; deduplication; 100 frames timed; TLS version and suite recorded; permission prompt and denial recorded. Design A also needs a forward-secret ECDHE-PSK suite on every pair. Design B uses proof-only certificates | Owner, X1 (harness test plan `X1`, `pair-session` with correlated IDs) | Production nearby transport (M6). **A release requirement:** a design is adopted only after its own proof passes on all nine cells; A failing never adopts B; if both fail, a redesign comes back as a plan revision, the gates stay open and the proofs repeat. X4 then verifies the production transport on the same cells |
| E-1 | Public fetch on Apple with Core unavailable | The X1 fetch probe (§8.9.3), including dual-stack, NAT64, an independently translated destination, IPv6 literals and redirects to them, and split-VPN paths, and interface-scoped `dns_sd` resolution and discovery with `requiredInterface` | Owner, X1 | Production retrieval design (M7). **A release requirement:** a failing platform keeps its gates open, and isn't released until a compliant redesign's proof passes there |
| E-2 | Apple SDK module maps and swift-crypto | Build the portable modules for macOS, iOS and iPadOS | Owner, X1 | Apple builds |
| E-3 | Production transport and enrollment on a device | `Apps/Probe` with LisaSyncHTTP, chain matrix | Owner, X2 | — |
| E-4 | First rootless container run | M2 harness, then the M4 image | Claude | — |
| E-5 | libxml2 2.15 and OpenSSL 4.0 compatibility | M0 build and suite | Claude | — |
| E-6 | `BGTaskScheduler` grants | Observed at X4 and X6 | Owner | — |
| E-7 | Local notification behavior | X6: a fired non-repeating request leaves the pending list; pending requests and the continuity marker survive a restart and a reboot; the stopped-app clock case | Owner | — |
| E-8 | Production retrieval and inference on all three devices | X5 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-9 | SELinux confinement | Target host | Owner | Container gate |
| E-10 | Sandboxed Mac: Keychain, listening, signing, export, import | X3, again at X7 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-11 | swift-certificates on devices (if Design B and P-08) | X4 | Owner | Apple gate |
| E-12 | An R1 Mac build from `491c90a` | Owner builds it at or before X3 | Owner | X7's genuine-app part |
| E-13 | Can Graph read calendars one personal Microsoft account shares with another, through the recipient's local copies, including several from one owner? (Microsoft's sharing pages don't mention personal accounts, §1.5) | LS-1: the owner shares two test calendars with "Can view all details" — one named like a calendar of the dedicated account — and a third with edit rights, and records the roles shown in Outlook; the dedicated account accepts them in an Outlook client; `OV-CAL-OUTLOOK-01` and `-14` find each shared calendar with `isSharedWithMe` and the owner's address, the two read-only ones with `canEdit` false and `canShare` false, refuse the editable one, read each selected window, and see an edit and a cancellation within the bound, each in its own calendar (§8.19.2) | Owner, LS-1; prerequisites: a dedicated Outlook.com account and an app registration in an Entra directory used only for verification (no owner commitment) | Outlook.com calendar row. A failure after the documented causes are checked keeps the row open; a failed lookup alone never justifies broader access, and authorizing the owner's own account would be the owner's decision about R2-SCOPE-02's boundary |
| E-14 | Does iCloud answer CalDAV with an app-specific password at `caldav.icloud.com`, and does a calendar shared privately and read-only to the dedicated Apple Account appear there without write privilege? (Apple documents neither, §1.5) | LS-1: `OV-CAL-ICLOUD-01` discovers the principal and the calendar home, lists the shared calendars, checks each one's privilege set, reads the window and records every property Apple returns | Owner, LS-1; prerequisites: a dedicated Apple Account with two-factor authentication and an app-specific password, no paid tier expected (to verify; no owner commitment) | iCloud Calendar row; if it fails, the row stays open with the evidence |
| E-15 | Does the official Bridge 3.27.2 run headless in the P-09a sidecar with an account: the interactive login with the service stopped, the keyring unlocked after a restart with a live session, the loopback listeners, the certificate from `cert export` pinned on IMAP and SMTP, automatic updates off, sending and receiving through the dedicated account? (The account-free startup checks are separate: `OV-MAIL-PROTON-17`, Claude, M7.) | LS-1 and LS-3 | Owner (login and live cases); prerequisites: P-09a (decided), a paid Proton plan, an amd64 host | Proton Mail rows |

### 10.6 Owner-run Apple sessions and live sessions

At each session the owner records, through the owner harness (§10.8): date; device models and OS versions; Xcode version; signing team and provisioning availability (including Push where relevant); permission states; and each result with its status, executor and cause, plus logs. Dates and results stay pending until then. Device availability is not verification: only a completed owner-run case supplies a physical or live result. Hardware supplies no APNs credentials, live authorization or production host.

| Session | After | Buildable entry point | Devices and accounts | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | M1 | `Apps/Proof` (nearby proof, fetch probe, proof-only certificates), the portable modules, and harness test plan `X1` | Mac, iPhone, iPad; all three pairs, each on shared Wi-Fi, with the Internet disconnected and with no access point | N-1 (both designs' proofs, the selection recorded only from a pass), E-1 (literals and redirects included), E-2 |
| X2 | M3 | `Apps/Probe`: a small signed app with LisaSyncEngine, LisaReplicaStore, LisaTrust and **LisaSyncHTTP**; test plan `X2` | iPhone and Mac (iPad optional), with a Core the owner runs from the M3 build | E-3: the chain matrix with leaf pinning; on every trust failure zero enrollment POSTs and no token disclosure (at most the token-free anchors GET); the Keychain retry item; a durable edit, sync, receipt lookup, camera denial |
| X3 | M5 | The full apps; test plan `X3` | Mac, iPhone, iPad | R2-A01, A02, A15; E-10; backup exclusion; export outside the container; the icon crop; E-12 (the R1 Mac build) |
| X4 | M6 | The full apps with the production nearby transport; test plan `X4` | All three pairs in the three conditions (nine cells) | R2-A03–A07 with the production transport of the selected design; E-6; E-11 if applicable |
| X5 | M7 | The full apps with retrieval and inference; test plan `X5` | **Mac, iPhone and iPad**, Core unavailable, controlled endpoints; the owner's own OpenAI and Anthropic keys and Ollama server for the live parts | E-8: permitted public GET (production path), disallowed destination refused before connection, AAAA-only name and IPv6-literal URL deferred, redirects to a private address and to a global IPv6 literal stopped, path change, the owner's split-tunnel VPN connected, authenticated model POST for each provider shape; the verified Core-unavailable case per platform |
| X6 | M8 | The full apps with notifications and Siri; test plan `X6` with the APNs preflight | iPhone, iPad, Mac; the owner's Apple Developer account, used only by the owner | R2-A09–A14, the stopped-app clock case after a restart and a reboot; E-7; the five APNs facts (§10.9) |
| X7 | M9 | Release-like builds, plus the R1 Mac build from E-12 or the fixture path; test plan `X7` | All three | R2-A16 (100 operations timed over an authenticated healthy foreground link, with hardware, payload, dataset and result recorded); A08; the Mac and companion upgrade rehearsal; S-13 device parts including the Mac helper and a late protected export on the active directory; E-10 again |
| LS-1 | M7 | `lisa-verify run --session LS-1 --mode live` on the owner's Mac, against the owner's Core | The dedicated Outlook.com, Gmail, Apple and Proton accounts; the owner's own calendars shared read-only to them | Identity binding and the non-personal wrong-account cases; mail ingestion and calendar reading for every provider (§10.9); E-13, E-14, E-15; every real action through the action ledger |
| LS-2 | M7, alongside X5 | `--session LS-2 --mode live` | The owner's OpenAI and Anthropic keys and self-hosted Ollama, on Core and on each device | Core inference and the Apple fallback for every provider (§10.9) |
| LS-3 | M8, alongside X6 | `--session LS-3 --mode live` | The dedicated mail accounts, the approved test recipient, the owner's Twilio account and designated test number | Outbound briefs and reminders per provider, found by their `Message-ID`; the live call with its four facts and the call log's count; uncertainty without a second call (§10.9); every real action through the action ledger |

### 10.7 Containers

The authorized rootless engine on this host is used from M2. The owner's server provides target-host evidence. Every result names the engine, context, host, architecture and image digests. arm64 is `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT`.

### 10.8 The owner-verification harness (O-6 extension, owner decision of 2026-11-22)

**What it is.** A tracked, versioned, comprehensive, package-level harness with which the owner verifies the Apple apps and the named services under the owner's own identity. It is a deliverable in its own right, authored separately from the temporary implementation harnesses (`CONSTRICTION.md`'s test rules; R2-DEV-02 "unless separately authorized"): it is written from this plan and the product's public interfaces, uses no file, code or data from `.tmp/`, and works from a clean checkout without private developer-machine state and without Claude operating any owner account. Ordinary development, sync, migration and recovery harnesses stay temporary in `.tmp/r2/<run-id>/` (O-6).

**Locations and entry points**
- **`Harness/`** (tracked):
  - `README.md`: the quick start on one page — prerequisites, then from a clean clone `swift run lisa-verify quickstart`, which builds the harness, creates `~/LisaVerify/config.json5` from the example, runs the preflight and lists the cases; then a first fixture run and a first owner-live run;
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyKit/`: the case model (ID, area, provider, interface, capability, environment, mode, required result level, required evidence, executor, prerequisites, gate, expected outcome, manual steps), the runner, the resumable run store with its **action ledger** (below), the result schema, reports, the redaction allowlist, the preflight checks and the **target checks** (below);
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyCases/`: the case catalogue, IDs `OV-<AREA>-<PROVIDER>-<NN>` (`OV-MAIL-GMAIL-07`, `OV-CAL-ICLOUD-12`, `OV-AI-OLLAMA-05`, `OV-CALL-TWILIO-03`, `OV-APPLE-X6-14`, `OV-NEAR-MI-03`, `OV-HOST-04`);
  - `Sources/LisaVerifyServers/`: the harness's own controlled provider-shaped servers for fixture mode — OAuth (Microsoft and Google shapes, including Google's Personal and External user types), Graph, Gmail API, Calendar API, CalDAV, IMAP and SMTP with a Bridge-like self-signed certificate, OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama, Twilio and APNs — labeled controlled and never contacting a service. Each **counts accepted submissions per logical action**, offers a lookup by the action's identifiers, **classifies every request it receives as identity-only, mailbox read, calendar read or send**, and can be told to accept and then drop the response, or to answer a rate limit (S-24);
  - `Sources/lisa-verify/`: the executable;
  - `Fixtures/`: synthetic mail (MIME variants, attachments, duplicates, identical messages without `Message-ID`, encoded headers), calendars (zones and DST changes, floating and all-day events, recurrence with moved and cancelled instances, multi-day events, several calendars shared by one owner), model transcripts (valid, invalid, refusal, truncated, usage variants), Twilio responses, and the **verification datasets** (`Fixtures/datasets/verify-<seed>`), generated by `lisa-verify fixtures generate --seed <n>` with recorded digests; no real personal data;
  - `compose/controlled.yaml`: the controlled servers and the controlled local Ollama container for the rootless engine; `compose/verification-core.yaml`: the **disposable verification Core** (below), whose Core service is named `lisa` like production's, so the same `deploy/lisa-deploy` wrappers drive it (§8.11.5);
  - `config/owner-verify.example.json5`: every platform and provider section with placeholders such as `<dedicated Gmail address>`, `file:<path to the app-specific password>`, `keychain:<item name>`; the list `protectedDatasets` of dataset IDs the harness must never touch (the owner's production Core); and, per OAuth provider, `verificationClients` (the client IDs registered only for verification) and `protectedClients` (the production Core's client IDs). Google additionally records owner-verified full client-ID to Cloud project-number mappings for each protected and verification client, the inventory of protected project numbers, the verification-only project and the confirmed dedicated account’s subject. The setup guide tells the owner to check the full client ID in that project's Google Cloud credentials screen and its project number in project settings. These mappings stay owner-local and the example uses placeholders.
  - `docs/`: `SETUP-APPLE.md` (Xcode, XcodeGen as R1 uses it, the team ID in the git-ignored `Apps/Config/Signing.local.xcconfig`, device registration, entitlements, profiles, the APNs key and token setup), one setup guide per provider (`SETUP-OUTLOOK.md`, `SETUP-GMAIL.md`, `SETUP-ICLOUD.md`, `SETUP-PROTON.md`, `SETUP-OPENAI.md`, `SETUP-ANTHROPIC.md`, `SETUP-OLLAMA.md`, `SETUP-TWILIO.md`) with account tier, client or bridge requirements, required scopes, a separate verification registration for Microsoft and a separate verification-only project for Google (§8.19.1), credential custody, limitations, propagation delays, the testable access path and the prerequisites table of §8.19; `HOST-STEPS.md`; `PAIRED-SESSIONS.md`; `CASES.md` (generated from the catalogue); `RESULTS.md` (the schema); `CLEANUP.md`.
- **`Apps/OwnerVerification/`** (tracked): XCUITest targets `LisaOwnerUITests-macOS` and `LisaOwnerUITests-iOS` (iPhone and iPad) that drive **the actual Lisa apps and their production code paths** through their UI, and one test plan per session (`X1`–`X7`) and area, added to `Apps/project.yml`. They run with `xcodebuild test -scheme <scheme> -testPlan <plan> -destination 'platform=iOS,id=<UDID>'`, or from Xcode. Steps no test can drive — radios, Focus, the lock screen, permission prompts, physical placement, setting the clock, hearing a call — are guided by `lisa-verify guide <case>` on the Mac, which shows each step with its expected outcome and records the owner's observation. App-side evidence comes from the production Diagnostics export (IDs, counters and states only) and, in owner-verification builds, a diagnostics panel listing Lisa's pending notification identifiers and the continuity marker (§8.10.4).
- **App entry points by session:** X1 uses `Apps/Proof` (scheme `LisaProof`: the nearby proofs of both designs, the fetch probe, the proof-only certificate encoder) with test plan `X1`; X2 uses `Apps/Probe` (scheme `LisaProbe`) with test plan `X2`; X3–X7 use the production apps (schemes `LisaMac` and `LisaCompanion`) with test plans `X3`–`X7`. `lisa-verify preflight apple` names the scheme, plan and destination for each device before a session.
- **Build dependencies:** the harness targets are declared in the root `Package.swift` with `path: "Harness/Sources/…"`, use only Foundation and the approved swift-argument-parser, SwiftNIO and swift-crypto, link the product modules they exercise, and are linked by no product; `Package.resolved` doesn't change. The Apple parts use Xcode, XCTest and XcodeGen, which the owner already uses for R1's apps.

**The disposable verification Core.** `lisa-verify core up --run <run-id>` starts a Core for one run in the owner's rootless engine, from the image or local build under test, with: a new data volume named `lisa-verify-<run-id>` and labeled `org.lisa.verification=<run-id>`; a fresh dataset created from a verification dataset (`--dataset verify-<seed>`), whose dataset ID and volume ID the run store records; the container label `org.lisa.verification=<run-id>`; its own administrative listener on the container's loopback; and its device listener on the address and port the owner names, reachable from the owner's devices. Its connector and delivery configuration names only the dedicated accounts with the **verification clients**, the approved test recipient and the test number from the harness configuration. `core down` stops it and leaves the volume for inspection until `cleanup`. The physical engine volume persists across these cases even when a restore creates a new logical volume ID. Identify it by the recorded engine/context, Compose project, volume name, creation fingerprint/labels and inspected mount. Bind the verification run to that resource. A recreated resource with a different fingerprint is a different target.

**Identity transitions survive a lost response.** Before initial start, restart or restore, the harness commits a pending operation with a fresh random ID, action, expected previous logical identity (or absent on designated first creation or fresh restore), dataset, immutable input/backup digest where applicable and physical target to its run store. Under the exclusive data-directory lock, the verification launcher checks the operation and records the expected transition in the target-local production intent on restore (in its launch record on start) after the locked target checks and before changing the dataset. Bind the resulting volume ID or new run generation to the same operation in the activation/boot record before the new generation can serve. Restart uses a fresh run generation; restore preserves its production rule for random logical volume allocation. Record the start receipt before serving; if a failed process never becomes available, close its recorded start and issue another recorded start on the same validated resource. Recovery uses the same durable operation ID: before activation, resume the pending action; after activation, reconcile the matching target receipt once, then verify the listener against that identity. A mismatch, a protected dataset, conflicting receipt, unexpected active generation, or unlabeled/replaced volume refuses. Never bless an unexpected ID simply because a listener or file now reports it. This recording is local to the verification run; the production restore intent in §8.5.1 supplies the activation receipt.

**Target checks before any step that changes or removes something.** A running Core can answer an authenticated check on its loopback listener; a stopped one can't. So there are two checks, and each host step uses the one that matches the state it starts from (the table below). Take the per-run host-operation lock, pin the exact engine resources, and recheck immediately before acting. Keep the run lock through the action and receipt commit. Stopped Core mutations also revalidate under the data-directory lock; initial start and restart use the recorded launch intent. Cleanup takes the offline check while exclusively controlling the stopped target, rechecks the exact engine resource after the checker exits, and uses ordinary engine removal which refuses a newly attached mount. Either check stops with "Target identity mismatch — nothing done" unless every item holds.
- **Running target** (Core up): (1) the container carries `org.lisa.verification=<this run-id>` and is running, and its data volume is `lisa-verify-<this run-id>`; (2) through `lisa-deploy admin` (§8.11.5), Core's loopback listener reports the dataset ID, volume ID and run generation this run recorded or reconciled from its bound start receipt; (3) that dataset ID isn't in `protectedDatasets`; (4) for a credential or grant step, the provider-side isolation check below.
- **Stopped target** (Core down, killed, or about to be restored, fenced or reversed): inspect the pinned engine/project and exact physical volume, its verification label and creation fingerprint; require no running service or other consumer. For restore, the host wrapper supplies the pinned physical identity, run intent and expected source dataset to the actual offline operation. Under the directory lock the command uses §8.5.1's explicit fresh, installed or matching interrupted state, rejects protected dataset IDs in the source or any predecessor, and compares the resulting receipt. A protected or foreign resource is refused even if apparently empty. For a stopped installed generation, the authoritative offline check takes the directory lock, opens the active generation through SQLite including WAL, and checks committed schema/fence, dataset and logical volume against the run store or the matching pending-operation receipt. Cross-check volume.json, reject protected datasets, busy/inconsistent stores, unexpected markers or another run’s identifiers. A files-only check may help diagnose a refusal. It cannot authorize an action.
- **R1 target** (an R1 data directory created by this run): the real directory and recorded filesystem identity belong to the run. Under R1’s offline lock, read the committed SQLite schema/fence with WAL and compare it with the expected R1 or prepared state. The actual migration/installation command rechecks before writing.

**Host steps, each mapped to its command mode** (§8.11.5). The owner runs `lisa-verify host-step <run-id> <case> <step>` in a shell on the Core host (directly, or in an SSH session the owner opens). It performs the step's target check, prints the exact command, runs it after the owner confirms, and records the redacted output against the case. `HOST-STEPS.md` lists every case's steps in this order, so each step starts in the state the previous one left, and none needs two states at once.

| Host step | Command | Mode and Core state | Target check | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kill | `docker kill` of the run's container | Running → stopped | Running target | Core host |
| Restart | `deploy/lisa-deploy up` for the run's Compose project | Stopped → running | Stopped target, persist restart intent, then reconcile the new start receipt and verify the running target | Core host |
| Stop for an offline step | `deploy/lisa-deploy stop` | Running → stopped | Running target | Core host |
| `core down` | `lisa-verify core down` (stop; keep the volume) | Running → stopped | Running target | Core host |
| Cleanup | `lisa-verify cleanup <run-id>`: remove the container, the run's volume and local run data | Stopped → removed | Stopped target | Core host |
| Restore, initial or resumed | `lisa-deploy offline restore <backupID> --operation <id> [--fresh] [--key-backup <dir>] [--reanchor]` | Offline, service stopped | Pin run and physical resource, persist intent, use the fresh/existing/interrupted check in §8.5.1, reconcile the activation receipt on a lost reply | Core host |
| Fence a previous writer's volume | `lisa-deploy offline fence --local --challenge <file> [--export-tail <dir>]`, against that volume's own Compose project | Offline; that writer stopped | Stopped target on that volume | That volume's host |
| Discover and ingest a backup | `lisa-deploy files backup scan --root <dir> --to <report>`, then `lisa-deploy admin backup ingest --report <report>` | Files, then listener | Selected backup root, then running target and explicit report | Core host |
| Review and settle held replay | `lisa-deploy admin trust replay <action> …` | Authenticated listener | Verified running target and command-specific recovery stage (§8.5.4) | Core host |
| `trust import-key` | `lisa-deploy offline trust import-key --file <key backup>` | Offline; stopped | Stopped installed target, with newly verified history for a later key import. A bootstrap-only key is supplied to restore in the preceding restore row | Core host |
| Rollback: preflight, fence, settlement | `lisa-deploy admin migrate rollback --preflight\|--fence\|--settle-legacy refuse-remaining` | Listener; running | Running target | Core host |
| Rollback: reverse | `lisa-deploy stop`, then `lisa-deploy offline migrate rollback --reverse` | Offline; stopped | Running target before the stop, stopped target after | Core host |
| Rollback installation | Linux uses the §8.14.7 Core commands. On the active Mac the owner runs the equivalent native `lisa-archive` commands for stage, verification on a disposable copy, then switch. | Stage and switch under the stopped target's R1 lock. Verification runs frozen R1 on the copy. | Exact R1 directory and staged three-file manifest | Active R1 host |
| Trust, fence codes and imports, inventory, legacy decisions, delivery review, lookup, confirm and release, connector authorize, revoke and pin, backup | `lisa-deploy admin <command>` | Listener; running | Running target (credential steps add the isolation check) | Core host |

**Provider-side isolation before a live grant change.** Apply the check at the verification Core's mutation boundary, to each interactive harness step, and before advising the owner to change a grant in the provider UI. This covers `OV-MAIL-*-10`, a grant-based `OV-CAL-*-02`, `connector revoke`, retries and any cleanup requesting a remote change. For a share-only test, confirm the verification calendar and recipient independently. Ordinary local-only cleanup can discard disposable tokens without any provider request.

For Google, require the existing confirmed binding for the dedicated Lisa account (which may use another OAuth project for production), verified issuer, audience and subject of its ID token, and the token's authorized party when present. Bind the credential actually selected for revocation to that issuer, subject, and full configured client ID through its successful identity-bound authorization and token-store entry. Match that client ID to the Cloud project number the owner verified in setup. Require a verification-only project with a complete owner-confirmed inventory excluding every protected project, including protected projects using other client IDs. Stop on an unproved client-to-project mapping, ambiguous grant origin, protected project, expired identity proof that cannot be refreshed, or unexpected subject. Token audience alone and the numeric prefix of a client ID do not prove project membership. Only an explicitly owner-selected operation on this proven disposable grant may reach the revocation endpoint. Record the result without tokens, raw subject identifiers, addresses or unredacted inventories. Blocked checks stay open with the missing prerequisite.

For Microsoft require the verification client's audience and the bound account's `oid` and `tid`; refuse a protected or shared client. For iCloud app-specific passwords and the Bridge login, have the owner confirm the verification credential's label or the Bridge account in the provider UI and record the observation before the step.

**Identifiers read from the owner's signed products.** The preflight reads the actual signed builds, never build settings: `codesign -d --entitlements - --xml` on each built `.app` for its entitlements (the platform APNs entitlement, keychain access groups, application groups, the App Sandbox on the Mac); the embedded provisioning profile (`security cms -D -i` on `embedded.mobileprovision`, or `Contents/embedded.provisionprofile` on the Mac) for its team, App ID, platform APNs entitlement, expiry and, for development profiles, the device list; and `CFBundleIdentifier` from the built `Info.plist`. On iPhone and iPad, use `xcrun devicectl device info apps` for the installed bundle identifier and version. On the Mac inspect the signed product actually launched and its local Info.plist. Match the signed product and profile using `com.apple.developer.aps-environment` on macOS and `aps-environment` on iOS and iPadOS. From these it derives, as R1's code does: the R1 Mac client directory `Application Support/<bundle identifier>/Client` (`Apps/Shared/Storage/ProtectedStorageLocation.swift`), inside the app's container when it is sandboxed, which holds the old queue the upgrade cases read; the Keychain service names (`Apps/Shared/Keychain/KeychainServiceName.swift`); and the APNs topics, which are the bundle identifiers. A mismatch between the signed entitlements and what a case needs — a missing platform APNs entitlement for a push case, an environment other than the Core's APNs configuration, a profile that doesn't list the device — stops that case before it runs. No private identifier is needed to plan or build the harness; the owner's values exist only in the owner's configuration and builds.

**Modes**
- **fixture** (default): deterministic fixtures and controlled servers; no account; runs on Fedora (Claude) and on the owner's Mac.
- **device:** owner-invoked; Apple builds on physical devices, signed and provisioned by the owner.
- **live:** owner-invoked; real providers under the owner's identity. Before running, it lists every **logical action** the selected cases will cause — each with its action ID, the message and its recipient, the notification, the model request with its estimated charge, or the call and its number — and needs `--confirm-real-actions <run-id>`, which records the digest of that list. It refuses any recipient or number outside the owner's approved test lists.

**The action ledger: no real action twice.** The run store keeps one record per logical action that can reach a person, a device, an account or a provider — a real message, a push, a model request, a Twilio call:
1. **Before any external I/O,** the harness commits the action's **intent**: its stable logical ID `OVA-<run-id>-<case>-<n>` (n is the action's position in the case's script, so the same action keeps its ID across resumes), its kind, target alias, the confirmation digest that covers it, and the Lisa identities it will use — the operation ID it will submit, and for a delivery the slot `(occurrence, firing, recipient)`, which Lisa's own journal follows (§8.10.6).
2. **Lisa performs the action, not the harness,** wherever the product does: the harness submits the operation (a reminder, a brief request, an inference request) and Lisa's production path sends, with Lisa's own claim, journal and holds. The harness records each identity as soon as it exists: Lisa's operation ID, delivery ID, `claimID` and `releaseID`, and the provider reference — the slot's `Message-ID`, the `apns-id`, the Twilio call SID, the model provider's request ID. A step the harness performs itself is marked `submitting` in its ledger before the first byte, as Lisa's journal does.
3. **Resume observes and reconciles; it never resends.** For each action without a recorded outcome, `lisa-verify resume <run-id>` observes, in this order: Lisa's state through the Core host's loopback listener (`lisa-deploy admin`, after the running-target check) or the app's diagnostics; then read-only provider evidence — Lisa's `delivery lookup` (Sent by `Message-ID` for Gmail, Outlook.com and Proton; Twilio's call log for the number and window, which may also show another call to that number), a device's receipt report; then the owner's observation. An action whose intent was recorded but whose operation never reached Lisa is resubmitted **with the same operation ID**, which Lisa deduplicates. An action that reached `submitting` with no outcome and no evidence found is **ambiguous**.
4. **Ambiguity needs a fresh, per-action authorization.** `lisa-verify authorize-retry <action ID>` shows the evidence and asks the owner to type "may duplicate <action ID>". It authorizes exactly one further attempt, recorded as `<action ID>.r2`, through Lisa's own one-attempt release where Lisa holds the slot (§8.10.6). Nothing else ever sends again.
5. **A run-level confirmation covers only the logical actions it listed.** After a crash, a resumed run may complete those actions; any action not in the confirmed list — including one a restarted case would create anew — needs a new `--confirm-real-actions` for the resumed run's own list. A confirmation never authorizes an additional attempt of an ambiguous action.
6. **What observation can establish, per kind:** an email by its `Message-ID` in Sent (absence proves nothing); a push by the device's receipt report and the owner's observation (APNs offers no lookup); a call by Twilio's call log for that account, number and window, which also gives the count of calls — a single call the owner heard can't establish how many were submitted, and a call in the log may be another call to the same number; a model request by Lisa's accounting record and the provider's request ID when the answer arrived, and otherwise it stays ambiguous, because neither OpenAI nor Anthropic offers an idempotency key or a lookup by request.

**Commands**
- `lisa-verify preflight apple|<provider>|all`: for Apple, the Xcode version, SDKs, signing identities (summarized, never exported), the signed products' entitlements and embedded profiles (above), connected devices and the installed apps' identifiers, the APNs key reference (a path, never the key), the topic matching the bundle ID, and Core's APNs environment matching the signed platform APNs entitlement and corresponding provisioning value — it states which profile will run; for a provider, placeholders replaced, the dedicated account's prerequisites (§8.19), the provider-specific verification identity and isolation evidence required for the selected case, the token store or password reference present, and, in live mode only, one read-only, identity-only authentication check shown before it runs.
- `core up|down`, `host-step`, `pair-session`; `run --case <id> | --area <area> | --session <X1…X7 | LS-1…LS-3> | --matrix full [--mode fixture|device|live]`; `resume <run-id>`; `retry <run-id> <case>`; `actions <run-id>` (the ledger); `authorize-retry <action ID>`; `guide <case>`; `observe <run-id> <case> --status pass|fail --observed "…"`; `import-diagnostics <run-id> <file>`; `report <run-id>`; `export <run-id> --redacted <file>`; `cleanup <run-id>`; `revoke-local <provider>`.
- **Unique IDs:** run IDs `run-<UTC time>-<6 base32 characters>`. Every synthetic resource a run asks the owner to create — calendar names and event titles, message subjects, prompts — carries `LISA-VERIFY <run-id> <case>`, so nothing collides with the owner's data, and cleanup finds only what the run made.
- **Repeatable and resumable:** the run store (`~/LisaVerify/runs/<run-id>/`, with `run.json` and the ledger `actions.sqlite`) records each case's state and the Core state each host step left; a run resumes after a crash or a pause; a single failed case reruns with `retry`, which creates new logical actions only under a new confirmation.

**Paired-device sessions (X1, X4, X7).** `lisa-verify pair-session <case> --devices mac,iphone|mac,ipad|iphone,ipad --condition shared-wifi|no-internet|no-access-point` prints, per device, the scheme, test plan and destination to launch (or the guided steps), and each device's **role** (sender or receiver). Every device's result carries the correlated case ID `<case>@<role>` (for example `OV-NEAR-MI-03@sender`) and a session nonce shown on both screens, so results from different devices join without a shared clock. **Timing:** each device records its monotonic clock; latency and the 100-operation timing are measured as round trips on one device, and a four-timestamp exchange over the session (20 exchanges, the minimum-delay sample) estimates the offset only for reporting one-way events. Wall-clock times are recorded for correlation, never for a measurement.

**Results**
- `results.jsonl`, one record per case: run ID, case ID, commit, harness version, provider and interface (for example "Gmail API", "Graph", "CalDAV", "IMAP via Bridge"), non-secret configuration (a digest plus allowlisted fields), platform, device model and OS version, Xcode version, APNs environment and profile kind, model ID where relevant, assumptions, required evidence, expected result, observed result, result level (§10.1), status, executor and exact cause, the action IDs and their outcomes, times, and evidence file names. A case recorded against a known finding (§17.1) names it.
- `summary.md`, owner-readable: per session and area, what passed, failed or waits, with causes and next steps.
- Manual observations, partial runs and single retries are recorded like any other result.
- **Redacted export:** `export --redacted` writes allowlisted fields only. It never contains service tokens, credentials, calendar URLs, mail content, prompts containing owner data, telephone numbers, provisioning material or raw push tokens; addresses and numbers become stable per-run aliases; provider references are kept only as digests. Planted canaries check the export (S-24).
- Results stay on the owner's machine (`~/LisaVerify/`), never in the repository. The owner shares whichever redacted exports they choose; Claude evaluates those, and only redacted evidence is attached to the project's reports.

**Owner resources, cleanup and revocation**
- Each setup guide lists the dedicated account to create, the verification registration and project-isolation requirements for that provider, the scopes or passwords to grant, the test calendars to create in the provider's own UI (two read-only plus the editable refusal control for calendar case 14), the approved test recipient and number, and the propagation delays to expect.
- `cleanup <run-id>` (after the stopped-target check) lists what the run created and removes what Lisa itself holds (the verification Core's container and volume, local token files, run data). Lisa's integrations are read-only, so provider-side items — test messages in the dedicated mailbox, the test calendars and their events — are removed by the owner in the provider's UI following `CLEANUP.md`, which names only `LISA-VERIFY <run-id>` items. `revoke-local` deletes the harness's local credentials with no remote grant call. Guides may direct the owner through a separate remote grant change only after the case-specific §10.8 isolation proof and explicit selection. Nothing unrelated is touched.
- **Faults that can't be induced safely or reliably against a real service** — token expiry after six months, provider outages, a 529, a rate-limit storm, an accepted request whose response is lost, a Bridge crash mid-fetch, a Twilio timeout after acceptance — run only against controlled servers and carry the Simulated class. Such a controlled case **closes its designated fault case** in §10.9; it never stands in for a live or physical case.

**Acceptance (S-24, every milestone from M1):**
- a fresh clone builds the harness and runs every fixture case without network access to any provider;
- **the action ledger against the counting controlled servers,** for an email, a push, a model request and a call: (i) killed after the intent and before any I/O → resumed, exactly one accepted submission; (ii) killed after the server accepted and before the harness recorded it → resumed by observation (the server's lookup), the outcome recorded, still one; (iii) the server accepts and drops the response, with its lookup disabled → ambiguous; resume sends nothing; `authorize-retry` without the typed phrase sends nothing; with it, exactly one more (two accepted, reported as a possible duplicate); (iv) a resumed run whose case would create a new action is refused until a new confirmation lists it; the old confirmation never covers it;
- a run interrupted mid-case resumes; one failed case reruns alone; a manual observation is recorded; a paired session's two device results join under one case and nonce;
- **host steps in their real modes, from a clean checkout, against the verification Core:** (a) **kill → restart:** the running-target check, `docker kill`, the stopped-target check (correct physical target; committed identity and schema/fence read through SQLite with WAL under lock), `lisa-deploy up`, the running-target check again with a new run generation; (b) **`core down` → cleanup:** the running-target check, stop, the stopped-target check, removal of the container and volume only; (c) **offline fence, restore and reverse:** each following stop (or a verified never-started target for fresh restore) and its action-specific stopped-target check, run through `lisa-deploy offline`, refused by the wrapper if Core is still running ("Lisa Core is running: stop it with `lisa-deploy stop` first"), and followed by the recorded state; `fence --local --challenge <file>` on a second run's volume, with that volume's stopped-target check; the rollback install on an R1 target with its R1 check; (d) **refusal against the wrong target:** a kill, a restart, a cleanup, an offline restore and a revocation aimed at a container or volume of another run, at a volume whose `volume.json` names another dataset or volume ID, at a dataset listed in `protectedDatasets`, at a volume whose lock is held, and at an R1 directory outside the run — each refused with "Target identity mismatch — nothing done", and the engine's and volume's state unchanged; a listener-mode step attempted while Core is stopped, and an offline step while it runs, each refused with the command that changes the state; no step in `HOST-STEPS.md` needs a state the previous step didn't leave;
  - **Lost transitions, WAL and schedule:** kill the harness after the target commits a restore ID and before the host stores it; retry reconciles one new volume/epoch. Kill after the verification start receipt but before the running check; retry never accepts a foreign generation. Repeat with a missing, old, wrong-run or mismatched operation receipt and a physically substituted volume: no mutation occurs. With checkpointing paused, leave main-file user_version 4 but commit the prepared fence marker in the WAL. Observe the committed marker and refuse a step expecting version 4; repeat after checkpoint with identical results, and require refusal for unreadable WAL/active lock. Run kill/restart and fresh, existing and interrupted restores from a clean M3 checkout through an evidence-only start and the first fence import, using the key-backup-only case in S-10. Rerun at M4 against its production image.
- **Provider grant isolation (S-24).** Two different Google client IDs in a single protected project for the same account must yield zero revocations. Repeat with a protected client, missing or contradictory project inventory, unknown grant origin, and unexpected subject. Test both wrong-account Google consent shapes and the protected existing binding. Exercise the direct admin route as well as the harness. An explicitly requested live-shaped case with a proven verification-only project, client and bound dedicated subject makes one revocation. Local cleanup always makes zero. On Microsoft distinguish protected/shared clients from the verification registration and check the bound account. Inspect the controlled request log for grant, mail, calendar and send traffic.
- **Signed entitlements.** Test both platform keys against signed products and matching profiles. The Mac push case checks `com.apple.developer.aps-environment`, and iPhone and iPad push cases check `aps-environment`. Exercise the correct key and matching environment, the other platform's key only, missing key, mismatched topic or environment and, where applicable, an unlisted device. Validate actual signed builds on all three owner platforms.
- the redacted export contains none of the canaries planted in configuration, prompts, mail, calendar URLs, numbers, provider references and push tokens;
- `cleanup` removes only what the run created; a delivery checklist confirms that nothing was taken from `.tmp/`.

### 10.9 Acceptance matrices: what "fully tested" means for the Apple apps and the named services

**Rules for every row.**
- A required row is **closed** only when every case it names has the result level **that case** requires, with the evidence it names, on every platform, pair, cell or provider it names (§10.1). An aggregate row never raises or lowers a case's level: it lists which of its cases close at `owner-verified`, which at `provider-accepted`, and which, as controlled fault cases, at `fixture-pass`. Until then it is open, with each pending case's status, executor and cause. Disabling an integration by default, or having no credential, never removes its row. A documented provider limitation keeps its row open.
- **Fault cases** that can't be induced safely against a real service — a rate limit, a quota error, a lost response after acceptance, a timeout after submission, a token expiry, an outage, an expired sync token — are closed by their **designated controlled case** at `fixture-pass` (Simulated class), and are marked "controlled" below. They never close a live or physical case.
- **Real-account and physical-device cases** close only with the owner's own result (`provider-accepted` where the row says so, otherwise `owner-verified`), recorded through the harness and shared, if the owner chooses, as a redacted export.
- **Known findings (§17.1).** For S-11 (l) and S-12 (m), record the actual observation and whether the known case was reproduced. A reproduced duplicate is a FAIL. A nonreproduction or unrun case leaves the finding open. Neither characterization closes a release requirement. The owner's deferral and later release review govern both findings.
- **Columns:** *Executor* is who runs the case; *Prerequisites* are what must exist before it can run (a milestone's code, a session, a dedicated account); *Status now* is the status on 2026-11-23 with its executor and cause; *Gate* is the release gate the row feeds (§16): G1 portable implementation, G2 container deployment, G3 Apple clients, G4 required integrations. Every row also feeds G5, the R2 release.
- **Status now, in every row below:** nothing has been implemented or run. Claude's fixture and harness cases are `BLOCKED_PERMISSION` (Claude; implementation awaits the owner's approval of this plan), then due at the named milestone. Owner cases are `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (owner; the named session is pending, and needs that milestone's build), and become `NOT_RUN_CREDENTIALS` (owner) if the preflight then finds a named prerequisite missing. Rows with another status say so.

| 5. Reconciliation | Core's state afterwards through `host-step`: the delivery's state, the reminder's state, and no second alert for the slot | Owner | `owner-verified` |

Refuse the editable calendar during discovery/selection before event reads and without issuing a write. Missing required shares leave that owner's case pending (Outlook at E-13; Google and iCloud the same way) | Claude → owner |

**Models, per provider, on Core and as the Apple fallback** (cases `OV-AI-<OPENAI|ANTHROPIC|OLLAMA>-NN`; fixture S-22 by Claude at M7 with controlled servers and the controlled local Ollama container; live LS-2 on Core and X5 on each device by the owner; gate G4, and G3 for the device parts)

| NN | Capability | Required evidence per case: fixture → live | Executor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Valid structured output for each R2-INT-02 workflow | Schema-valid results → on Core, Mac, iPhone and iPad | Claude → owner |
| 02 | Invalid output → one repair, counted | Controlled only | Claude |
| 03 | Refusal and truncation → invalid result | Controlled only | Claude |
| 04 | Unavailable model | Fixture → a wrong model ID | Claude → owner |
| 05 | Authentication refused | Fixture → a revoked test key (no charge); Ollama: the proxy token | Claude → owner |
| 06 | Rate limits, quota and overload | Controlled only | Claude |
| 07 | Usage recorded for the budget | Fixture usage → compared with the provider's own report | Claude → owner |
| 08 | Timeout after possible submission → `uncertain`, never resent; the harness's ambiguous action needs `authorize-retry` | Controlled only | Claude |
| 09 | Restart without duplicate spending | Kill injected → the app force-quit mid-request | Claude → owner |
| 10 | Device-local consent and its withdrawal | Fixture → on each device | Claude → owner |
| 11 | Core-unavailable workflows on Mac, iPhone and iPad | Fixture → X5 | Claude → owner |
| 12 | Endpoint policy: public DNS; the self-hosted endpoint on a private literal with a pin, for its model route only; loopback `http` on the Mac | Fixture → X5 and LS-2 | Claude → owner |

The controlled local Ollama container gives `fixture-pass` with the real protocol; only the owner's own Ollama deployment gives `owner-verified`.

**Calls (Twilio)** (cases `OV-CALL-TWILIO-NN`; fixture S-23 by Claude at M8; live LS-3 by the owner; gate G4)

| NN | Capability | Required evidence per case: fixture → live | Executor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Dry run and contract faults (invalid number, refused create, timeout) | Controlled → optional with the owner's test credentials, which place no call | Claude → owner |
| 02 | Approved number only; others refused before any call | Request log → nothing placed | Claude → owner |
| 03 | Urgency, acknowledgement, quiet hours and rate controls | Controlled only | Claude |
| 04 | **A live call to the designated test number, four facts recorded separately:** acceptance (201 and the call SID, `provider-accepted`); lifecycle (the polled statuses to a final one); receipt (the owner's phone rang and the owner answered); and the spoken content (the owner heard the expected phrase and the reference) | The scripted server's progression → each fact by the owner; `owner-verified` only with all four | Claude → owner |
| 05 | **Total submissions** for the case's window: Twilio's call log for that number counts the calls; a single call the owner heard can't establish it, and a call in the log may be another call to the same number, which the owner identifies | The counting server → `delivery lookup` on the owner's account | Claude → owner |
| 06 | Suppression for ineligible, expired or already-handled events | Fixture → nothing placed | Claude → owner |
| 07 | Uncertainty after submission, restart or restore → no automatic second call | Controlled kill after acceptance → Core killed after the create through `host-step`; the call log shows exactly one call | Claude → owner |

**Container and target host** (gate G2): the rows of §8.11 and §10.7 — rootless amd64 D-checks (Claude, from M4); target-host D05, VPN and off-VPN over IPv4 and IPv6, SELinux (`OV-HOST-*`, owner); arm64 build and run `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` (Claude; no arm64 environment — the 376 debug and 376 release passes on Debian 13/aarch64 are R1's, from its retired environment), so G2 can pass for amd64 only and is reported that way.

**What remains open now (2026-11-23):** every row above. Proton Calendar is the only row that can't close under the current requirement, because the provider interface is missing (§8.19.6). The known findings KF-1 and KF-2 (§17.1) stay open alongside the rows, deferred by the owner, for the release review.

## 13. Commits and buildable checkpoints

- **One file-level operation per commit,** staging only that file. Implementation and documentation are committed separately; messages are `add: …`, `change: …` or `remove: …`. `git status` is checked before every commit, and changes Claude didn't make are never staged.
- **Ordering keeps the tree buildable:** new files before their callers, additive changes before removals, protocol additions with default behavior before conformances.
- **When a change can't be ordered that way** (a signature change touching several files), the commits form a span:
  - the span's last commit is the **buildable checkpoint**, built and tested on the working tree before the next span starts;
  - `R2-CHECKPOINT.md` records the span's first and last commit and the checkpoint's HEAD with the build result.
  - Earlier commits are never checked out to test them, because checkouts aren't permitted (§3).

## 14. Checkpoint and resume (R2-DEV-03; O-7)

- **The record:** a tracked `R2-CHECKPOINT.md`, beginning at M0. It holds:
  - branch and baseline; current milestone; completed requirement IDs;
  - modified and uncommitted files;
  - commands and results, with evidence locations;
  - open conflicts and blockers; pending sessions and experiments;
  - permissions in force;
  - the last buildable checkpoint;
  - the next steps.
- **Updates:** at every milestone boundary and before any pause. Nothing is reverted to look clean.
- **On resume:** the actual files and processes are inspected first.
