## 17. Risks

- **Scale.** Much of the Apple code can only be syntax-checked here. Mitigation: portable logic first, thin adapters, early X1–X3, the shared transport proved at X2, and owner-harness runners delivered before each session.
- **Nearby security.** Design A may fail N-1; Design B needs P-08 for production certificates. A design is adopted only after its own proof passes, so a failure of both means a redesign and repeated proofs, with the gates open meanwhile; X4 can still fail the production transport.
- **Apple retrieval** may fail E-1 on a platform. E-1 is a release requirement: the client defers to Core as a runtime fallback, the gates stay open, and a compliant redesign is proved. Names with only AAAA records are always deferred to Core on Apple devices (§8.9.3).
- **Rootless versus target host.** Networking, UID mapping and SELinux differ, so those checks repeat on the target.
- **Fedora drift:** libxml2 2.15, OpenSSL 4.0 (E-5), Swift 6.3.2 versus 6.4.2.
- **Recovery depends on owner actions:** stopping or fencing every other host and every copy of its volume, keeping off-host copies of the recovery ledger and backups, backing up secret files, and collecting trust bundles from devices whose keys changed after a backup. The documentation and the restore review ask for each.
- **Owner attestations are trusted as stated.** The writer inventory's completeness, the number of copies of a volume and an attested stop can't be verified by a Core that can't reach the old host, and an attested restart prevention isn't enforced by Lisa. Mitigation: Lisa refuses an inventory missing any candidate it knows; every copy is its own entry; a documented search checklist and stop procedure; a code-bound `fence --local` whenever the old host is reachable, which enforces restart refusal by marker on that copy, binds the journal's end and exports the tail; evidence records that say which kind each fence is; authenticated provider outcomes with independent journal attribution when available; containment the moment another writer's evidence appears. Client isolation and credential rotation are never fences.
- **A lost or merely attested volume leaves deliveries held.** Without a fence that binds the old writer's final journal entry, every slot whose deliverable window overlaps that writer's possible activity stays held until evidence closes it, the owner settles it or it expires; an email without `validUntil` never expires and waits for the owner. Provider lookups help only where the provider keeps a Sent copy or a call log, and a call log can't tell which writer placed a call (§8.10.6).
- **Two known findings stay open** (§17.1): a restored Core can still send a reminder twice (KF-1), and an Apple notification update can alert twice (KF-2). The owner deferred both on 2026-11-23 without waiving a requirement; the release review decides.
- **Reconciling a lost task create needs the owner** whenever R1's own ledger record is gone; R1 offers no other identity for a task (§8.14.8).
- **Rollback requires the active host.** The owner stops R1 and the installer takes R1's directory lock. Linux uses the Core CLI. The Mac uses the native archive helper for activation and later exports (§8.14). M9/X7 determines whether the actual frozen Mac host and helper can complete the procedure. A missing local path keeps activation pending.
- **Provider access can change.** Google's Personal user type, Microsoft's directory requirement for app registrations, Apple's undocumented CalDAV endpoint and the Graph route for shared personal calendars (E-13, E-14) are outside Lisa's control. Mitigation: each is checked by the owner at LS-1 and documented in its setup guide; a failure keeps its row open and, where another route would change the trust model, comes to the owner. For Google, the owner proves the dedicated subject, the actual token's origin and a verification-only Cloud project separate from all protected projects before a test can revoke a grant (§10.8). Wrong-account rejection discards provisional credentials locally. Missing proof keeps that live case pending.
- **Proton:** Proton Calendar has no compliant route; under the owner's settled policy its row and the release gate stay open until Proton adds an interface, which is re-checked on a schedule (§8.19.6). Proton Mail runs through the P-09a Bridge sidecar, which runs only on 64-bit x86, so an arm64 Core host can't serve Proton Mail with the official package; a headless Bridge isn't a configuration Proton documents, so E-15 decides it, and whether its listeners serve without an account is recorded at M7 (§8.19.5).
- **Identity binding rests on one owner confirmation.** The first binding of a dedicated account relies on the owner confirming the address shown; afterwards the stable identifier is enforced. Google's Personal user type adds a provider-side refusal of other accounts; its External type and Microsoft have none, so only Lisa's binding refuses them.
- **Zone-dependent source coverage.** While an affected source's civil inputs or coverage proof is incomplete, affected queries can lose free-time suggestions until Core re-expands the source. This includes March 28 in the cross-midnight gap case. Offline and newly requested days use the same rule. All-day dates remain readable in the planning zone (§8.4.2, S-08).
- **Re-anchoring needs owner work for every inherited entry, and fresh fence evidence for every inventory entry;** an entry no device can prove stays unusable until retired (§8.5.6).
- **A reviewed-set artifact that never arrives** finalizes as expired after 14 days, leaving the conflicts for a new resolution (§8.3.2).
- **Model APIs change quickly:** sampling parameters dropped by newer models, Anthropic's dated API versions, new structured-output rules. Mitigation: the version and model profile are configuration, adapters reject unknown answers as invalid rather than guessing, and the owner's LS-2 session re-verifies against the current services.
- **The owner harness must stay honest as the product changes.** Mitigation: it builds from a clean checkout at every milestone, its fixture mode runs in CI-like fashion on Fedora, its host steps check their target in the state each starts from, and its redaction is tested with canaries (S-24).
- **Blobs inside SQLite** grow the database and its backups; staging, promotion and WAL growth are bounded per transaction (§8.16.3) and measured at M2 and M3.
- **Inherent limits, documented:**
  - revocation reaches a device only when it learns of it;
  - an isolated device's only copy can be lost;
  - exactly-once delivery is impossible, and released holds can duplicate; beyond that, KF-1 and KF-2 are open findings, not accepted limits;
  - handoffs and the 32-request cap (31 firings plus the continuity marker) can leave coverage holes;
  - an alert the OS showed and the owner cleared while Lisa was closed can't be proven seen, so without continuity evidence it stays `outcome unknown` and isn't shown again unless the owner asks; with it, Lisa records the firing as done, which is no receipt;
  - deleted content leaves the raw database and WAL files only at a successful TRUNCATE checkpoint, which a long-lived reader delays; below the file system nothing is claimed (§8.6.7);
  - RFC 7050 can't learn range-specific prefixes, so AAAA answers are never used on Apple devices;
  - per-device model budgets aren't a global cap;
  - R1 has no conditional preference set for a present key, and no conditional confirm or delete, so the post-downgrade tool applies them only under R1's own lock with R1 stopped, or leaves them to the owner;
  - a model request whose answer was lost can't be looked up at the provider, so another attempt needs the owner's explicit authorization.
- **Migration** is verified on synthetic data only. R1's Apple apps may not build (E-12).
- **Owner availability:** X1–X7 and LS-1–LS-3 need the owner's time; N-1 and E-1 also gate production designs.

### 17.1 Known findings register

Findings the owner has reviewed and deferred. Each stays **open** and visible in the matrices (§10.9), in release reporting (`IMPLEMENTATION-REPORT-R2.md`, §12) and in §22 until the release review decides it. Deferral isn't a waiver: no release requirement is relaxed, and no case below is counted as passing. No fix is designed in revision 8, by the owner's decision; the existing protections stay in force for the cases they cover.

| | **KF-1 A restored Core can still send a reminder twice** | **KF-2 An Apple notification update can alert twice** |
|---|---|---|
| Status | Open; deferred by the owner on 2026-11-23; no waiver | Open; deferred by the owner on 2026-11-23; no waiver |
| Trigger | A backup holds a reminder due at 14:00. After the cut, the previous Core moves that firing to 10:05 — a new generation of the same firing (§8.10.1) — and sends it at 10:05; then its journal tail is lost. The restored Core evaluates the same firing from the backup's schedule: its deliverable window, 14:00 to 18:00, doesn't overlap the previous writer's possible-submission interval (the cut to the writer's stop plus σ), so no hold applies (§8.10.6), and at 14:00 it may send again. Separately, an unobserved jump of the previous writer's clock — one no surviving heartbeat records — can put an earlier submission outside the inferred interval | Lisa reads the OS pending list and finds a request pending. Before Lisa adds the replacement under the same identifier (a new generation, §8.10.4), the OS delivers the original. The add then schedules the replacement as a new request, which alerts again |
| Visible consequence | The owner receives the reminder twice: by email (the same slot `Message-ID`, so a provider lookup would find the first — but nothing prompts one, because the slot isn't held), by push, or by a call | The owner is alerted twice for one reminder: the original, then its replacement |
| Affected sections | §8.10.6 (intervals, windows, holds), §8.10.1 (firing identity and generations), §8.5.1 (attribution), §8.19.4 (calls) | §8.10.4 (replacement, reconciliation precedence), §8.10.5, §8.13 |
| Affected cases | S-11 (no case covers the moved-earlier firing; (l) attempts to reproduce it), N-A (fires M at its original time, so it doesn't show this), `OV-APPLE-X6-07`–`12`, `OV-CALL-TWILIO-05` | S-12 (no case covers the race; (m) attempts to reproduce it), `OV-APPLE-X6-07`–`12` (R2-A10), X6 |
| What the current evidence supports | Holds stop a second submission of every slot whose deliverable window, as the restored Core derives it, overlaps a previous writer's possible-submission interval, and of every slot with a journaled start: the overdue reminder, open claims, a release and its crash, credentials outliving revocation (S-11 (a)–(k)). Nothing supports "no repeat delivery after a restore" beyond those cases | Exact-target matching, OS intents and crash precedence stop removals and replacements of the wrong firing and re-adds after a crash or a clock step (S-12, the stopped-app clock case at X6). A surviving continuity marker shows only that the OS store wasn't reset; it establishes no receipt or display of the individual reminder. Nothing supports "no second alert on an update" |
| Related limitation kept | A Twilio call log match on number and time may be another call to the same number, so it never attributes a call to a writer or settles a slot by itself (§8.10.6) | Kept distinct: the stopped-app clock case (an alert shown and cleared while Lisa was closed) and the owner's deliberate snooze and **Show again**, which create f+1 by design (§8.10.4) |
| Characterization (future evidence) | S-11 (l) at M3 records the actual submission count and provider evidence. Mark a reproduced duplicate FAIL; otherwise record the observation. KF-1 stays open. | S-12 (m) at M8 records the actual alerts from the simulated interleaving. Mark a reproduced duplicate FAIL; otherwise record the observation. KF-2 stays open. |
| Gates kept open | G4 and G5 (R2-CORE-06; R2-NOT-06; R2 §18.2 "Retried operations do not duplicate data or deliveries") | G3 and G5 (R2-A10; R2-NOT-05) |

## 18. Stopping conditions

Work on an affected item stops only when:
- an action needs authorization outside the approved scope;
- an enforced platform restriction needs the owner;
- a concrete technical blocker can't be resolved within scope;
- credentials, Apple sessions, the target host or live validation are needed — the owner's to run, never Claude's;
- a provider lacks a capability a requirement needs, which is reported with primary-source evidence and the affected gate (§22);
- a deciding experiment hasn't run where its result is a stated prerequisite.

Independent work continues, and each blocked operation is reported with its exact cause.

## 19. End-to-end acceptance narratives

Both narratives are proposed checks. They run as portable harnesses with synthetic data, scripted providers and, before M8, a scripted device client: N-A at M3, its container part at M4, and its delegated-firing step again at M8 with the real device side; N-B at M9, with device parts at X7. Each step lists the stage, the evidence available, what is allowed, and what the owner sees. Every component a step uses exists at the milestone that runs it (§9). Each narrative runs its steps in the order of §8.5.1's sequence and §8.14.7's installation; no step needs a state or an authority that a later step creates.

### 19.1 Sibling restores, a writer missing from Lisa's records, a previous writer cut off but still writing, an overdue reminder after a lost tail, a rotated key, a device key Core doesn't know, a newer revocation, shared data and provider acceptances (N-A)

**Setup.** Epoch E1 runs under Core key K1 on host H1, with a Mac, an iPhone, an iPad and a fourth device C enrolled, and `retainRestorable = 2`. Every email carries its slot's `Message-ID` and the sending volume's `Lisa-Writer` header. Then:
1. H1 was down from 07:30 to 09:58. Use a virtual clock and pause only H1's dispatch/recover worker through step 6, leaving other fixture operations available. M is due at 08:00, its push valid until 12:00 (240 minutes) and email until the 18:00 task deadline. Backup A at 10:00 is validated against the owner key-backup anchor, binds its journal prefix at 10:00 and contains no submission for M.
2. Core rotates K1→K2, records the signed transition in its database and recovery ledger, and the fixture owner saves K2 separately to the key backup.
3. The owner revokes C with a K2-signed roster.
4. The iPhone commits X. A large capture Y1 commits with blob b. Core delegates firing 1 of P to the iPhone, which schedules it locally.
5. Take valid backup B at 10:08. It contains X and Y1; R = {A, B}. Both cuts precede M’s first submission.
6. At 10:09 copy A, B and the recovery ledger/journal prefix off-host. Record each chain/sequence/hash. Keep a good B for later discovery and separately corrupt the initial B copy. Prove all good copies of A, B and the portable journal prefix lack M’s submission and receipt. No later H1 journal copy is available in the lost-tail branch.
7. At 10:15 resume the worker. H1 fires M. The scripted Gmail-shaped provider accepts with the slot’s Message-ID, recipient, H1’s Lisa-Writer header and harness-recorded provider reference; scripted APNs accepts its push, which the iPhone shows. H1’s durable journal links its claim and the provider reference. The external test oracle retains the events, separately from all evidence supplied to recovering Core.
8. The iPad rotates its device key (D1→D2); Core accepts the key update and publishes roster v9 (K2) naming D2. The iPad then goes offline and saves edit Y2, which references blob b. Only H1's volume and the iPad hold v9.
9. The scripted SMTP server accepts an email reminder for slot O (firing 1). H1's journal records it; the off-host copy doesn't.
10. H1 fails at 10:40 and is lost with its volume.

| # | Step and stage | Evidence available | Allowed action | What the owner sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Restore on fresh H2 (stage 0) | A is anchored. The mounted off-host ledger holds the verified K1→K2 transition, C's revocation and B's index. B's off-host copy fails validation. Only the owner's separately mounted key backup supplies the K2 secret | `lisa-deploy offline restore A --operation H2-A --fresh --key-backup <dir>` on the owner-designated empty target checks that K2 is current, loads the matching key, then signs E2 and activates stage 1. The owner starts H2's evidence-only service with the same K2 secret provisioned. Its candidate list names H1 | “Restored A. Excluded B because its digest failed. Epoch E2 signed with K2 from your key backup. Candidate H1 awaits inventory.” |
| 2 | Fence H1 (stage 1) | H1 and its disks are destroyed | Using the running stage-1 listener, the owner inventories H1, attests completeness, follows the documented procedure and attests `owner-attested-stopped` for H1's entry (volume destroyed, stopped at 10:40). The evidence record says "restart prevention attested by the owner, not enforced". H1's possible-submission interval is recorded per channel as (10:00 − σ, 10:40 + σ]. No fence binds H1's journal | "Previous Lisa Core on H1: stopped (attested by you, 10:40; restart prevention attested, not enforced by Lisa); its journal can't be checked. Delivery suspended." |
| 3 | Evidence collection and staging (stage 1) | iPhone and Mac authenticate by challenge with keys from A's roster and upload K2-signed rosters, the K1→K2 record, signed inventories listing receipted X and Y1, including the binding to Y1’s required blob b and assignment inventories (P scheduled on the iPhone). C's upload is quarantined once the merged evidence revokes it. The iPad is offline | Collect and stage; nothing applied | `trust review`: "Device C: revoked (<time>, K2). iPad: not yet reported. Mac, iPhone: active. Did any device enroll or change its key after <A's time>? Collect its trust records before confirming." |
| 4 | Confirm (stage 2) | H1's writer fence is closed; no trust bundle or staged item is pending | `trust confirm` writes confirmation n+1 for E2, signed with K2, fixing manifest entries X, Y1 and b, with bytes outstanding. Devices accept E2 only now (a confirmed head that descends from E1 at A's cut) and refresh their credentials | "Membership confirmed; replaying your devices' changes next." |
| 5 | Replay (stage 3a), then ordinary work (stage 3b) | iPhone and Mac bodies for X and Y1, retained because A doesn't contain them, carrying E1 receipts; blob b, because Y1 holds it | 3a waits for and verifies the listed X, Y1 and b, then replays X and Y1 in dependency order; the replay report complete, 3b opens, and the scheduler derives plans and reminder schedules again | "Replayed 2 changes. Not yet heard from: iPad." |
| 6 | Discover B (stage 3b) | A good copy of B from the same catalogued H1 writer appears | Run `lisa-deploy files backup scan --root <dir> --to <report>`. Observe unchanged live stage. Then `lisa-deploy admin backup ingest --report <report>` streams B, rechecks its anchored manifest and known writer, verifies eligible items under E2 and deduplicates X and Y1. Retry after a lost receipt. B’s journal predates M | “Backup B verified. 2 operations already applied. No new writer.” |
| 7 | Second restore while H2 runs, with every witness of H2 offline (stage 0 → 1 on H3) | H2's volume is misconfigured, so the owner restores A again on H3 (E3) from the off-host copies, whose ledger **predates H2's restore**. The Mac committed edit Z on H2 earlier and holds H2's receipt, but the Mac and the iPhone — the only devices that know H2 — are switched off. Lisa's list on H3 therefore names only H1. H2 keeps running: at 07:00 its scheduler recomputes the plan and syncs reminders, and at 07:05 a script the owner left on H2 adds task Z2 through H2's local CLI. The owner removes H2's VPN route (`fence note --client-isolated`), rotates the APNs key and the SMTP password, and revokes the dedicated Gmail account's grant (`fence note --credentials-rotated`) | Stage 1 only. H3 can't close its inventory from its own records: `trust confirm` and v2, v1 and CLI writes are refused, the scheduler and outbox stay stopped, until the owner's inventory is attested. The owner adds "restore of A on H2" (entry `W-3K7Q2D`) and attests; H2's writer fence is `unknown`, so stage 2 stays closed. The isolation note, the rotations and the revocation change nothing about writes, and Google documents no bound for the revocation, so it ends no channel | "List every place this data could run, each copy separately, and attest the list before Lisa can accept changes." Then: "Previous Lisa Core on H2 is cut off from your devices (noted <time>) but may still change data on its own: its schedule, connectors and local commands. This Lisa Core accepts no changes until H2 is stopped with restart prevented. APNs and SMTP: credentials rotated; Gmail grant revoked — information only." |
| 8 | Fence H2 and stage its tail (stage 1) | The owner reaches H2's console | `lisa core fence code W-3K7Q2D --out <file>` on H3 writes the portable A-cut challenge and displays the separate code. The owner carries the file to H2, checks the H3 fingerprint independently, and supplies the code by prompt. The owner stops H2's Core and runs `lisa core fence --local --challenge <file> --export-tail <dir>` on H2's volume (offline there): it takes the lock, writes the schema marker and a terminal journal record, signs a fence record binding H2's final run, journal sequence and head, with the code's MAC, and exports what H2 committed after A's cut, Z2 included, with its retained-evidence report (every range exact). `fence import` on H3 verifies the MAC and records `fenced-on-host`, restart refusal "enforced by marker"; the tail is **staged**: verified against the fence record's bound hash and stored in quarantine, **nothing applied**. H2's restart policy then fires, and H2's Core refuses to open the fenced database | "Previous Lisa Core on H2: fenced on its own host (<time>); restarts refused by its volume marker; its journal ends at entry 4,812 and can't continue. Its changes after backup A are staged for your confirmation (all as exact changes): 1 task, H2's plans and reminder schedules." |
| 9 | Confirm E3 (stage 2) | The inventory (H1 attested stopped, H2 fenced on host), the ledger copy on H3, device reports of E2's head once the Mac and iPhone are back, H2's staged tail | Confirmation n+2 for E3 with `supersedes: [E2]`, its evidence digest fixing the replay set: H2's staged tail and the devices’ signed inventories naming receipted X, Y1 and Z and their dependencies. The Mac and iPhone verify the confirmed sibling supersession and refresh credentials | Mac and iPhone: "Lisa Core was restored again; resending your changes." Owner: "E2 superseded. Next: replaying H2's staged changes and your devices' changes." |
| 10 | Replay (stage 3a, restricted) | The replay set; the devices' bodies X, Y1 and Z, carrying E2's receipts | Z2 replayed from the tail; X, Y1 and Z resubmitted by the Mac and iPhone and applied once; H2's submission journal merged as delivery evidence; plans, briefs and reminder schedules marked for re-derivation. A new iPhone capture without a superseded-epoch receipt gets `trust_recovery` (stage `replay`) and stays in its journal; no scheduler, connector or delivery runs. Core is killed halfway through the replay and restarted: it resumes at the replay cursor, and nothing is applied twice | Owner: "Replaying: 3 of 4 changes applied." Then "Replayed from H2: 1 task added on H2. Replayed from your devices: 3 changes." |
| 11 | Ordinary work (stage 3b) | The replay report: every item decided | Ordinary intake reopens. At M3 the fixture issues a fresh, H3-only scoped credential for the same dedicated scripted mailbox. Verify the bound account identity, required scopes and authenticated Sent access. The full consent and account binding are tested at M7 with the owner harness. The revoked H2 grant is denied by the script. Install H3-only replacement SMTP and APNs credentials for later channel resumption. No production accounts or credentials participate; the iPhone's capture is accepted. The scheduler, after holds and intervals reload, derives H2's derived writes again, then runs E3's `recover`, which regenerates M's push and email slots at 11:05; both are held at creation, because their windows (08:00–12:00 and 08:00–18:00) overlap H1's interval | "Lisa Core is accepting changes. Delivery is still suspended until you review it." |
| 12 | The iPad returns late, with a key Core doesn't know (stage 3b) | The iPad holds E1's head, Y2 pending, roster v9 naming its key D2, and a bearer the restore invalidated. E3's roster still names D1 | Its first request gets `credential_invalidated_by_restore`; its refresh challenge is refused because D2 is unknown. The owner runs `lisa core trust evidence-code`; the iPad uploads its bundle (v9 and the K1→K2 record); Core verifies v9 from A's anchored key history through K1→K2. D2 becomes known; the iPad's challenge succeeds; it refreshes, verifies E3 (confirmed, descending from E1 at A's cut), receives the roster revoking C, and submits Y2; blob b is already present | iPad: "Lisa Core doesn't recognize this device's current key. Ask for a code to send this device's trust records." Owner: "Trust records from iPad: roster v9 verified; iPad's current key accepted." Then "iPad reported; 1 change admitted." |
| 13 | C tries again | C is revoked in the merged roster | Its operations after the revocation are rejected; operations with same-lineage receipts from before it are admitted | "Device C: 1 change rejected (revoked), kept for inspection." |
| 14 | Delivery review (stage 4) | E3's database lacks O's email row and P's assignment, and holds M's regenerated slots as held. H1's journal is lost, the off-host copy ends before O's acceptance and M's firing, and H1's attested fence binds no journal entry. H2's fence binds its final entry; its complete journal shows one accepted brief email (slot Q), nothing for O or P, and M held (H2's own restore held it too). The iPhone has reported P as scheduled locally and M's 10:15 alert in its delivered list | `delivery resume` resumes SMTP and APNs with every hold kept. O stays held: no completeness proof exists for H1. Q stays accepted and is never resent. P becomes device-owned and is never pushed. **M:** the push slot closes on the iPhone's report; `delivery lookup M` finds M's `Message-ID` in the dedicated account's Sent folder on the Gmail-shaped server, with `Lisa-Writer` naming H1's volume and a provider send time of 10:15, which is consistent with H1's attestation. The authenticated Sent record matches the slot and recipient, so the slot closes as submitted, writer unverified; nothing is resent. H1's lost journal prevents attribution, and the header alone triggers no containment. Core is killed and restarted during the review; every hold, interval and closure is reloaded first. **O:** the owner runs `delivery release O` with acknowledgement; the released attempt's answer is lost in a crash, so O becomes `outcomeUnknown`; the owner finds the reference in the inbox and records `delivery confirm O --received` | "Held: email reminder for <task> — the previous Lisa Core on H1 could have sent it between 10:00 and its stop; no fence binds its last journal entry. Sending again may duplicate it." For M: "Reminder 'Water the plants' (due 08:00): already sent at 10:15; writer unverified (found in the dedicated mailbox's Sent folder; shown on iPhone). Not sent again." After the crash: "Released by you; outcome unknown. Reference R-7Q4K: check your inbox for it." After the owner's record: "Delivered (confirmed by you)." |

**Checks at the end:**
- H3 couldn't promote on Lisa's own list, which lacked H2: it waited for the owner's attested inventory, and then for H2's code-bound fence. It confirmed nothing, scheduled nothing, delivered nothing and admitted no write while H2's writer fence was unknown, although H2 was cut off from every client, the APNs and SMTP credentials had been rotated and the Gmail grant revoked.
- H2's tail was staged before the confirmation and applied only at stage 3a, after the confirmation fixed the tail and the devices’ declared entries and dependencies as the replay manifest; nothing ordinary was admitted before 3b, and nothing delivered before stage 4.
- **Restarts:** Core killed on H3 after the tail was staged (step 8), after the confirmation (step 9), halfway through the replay (step 10) and just after 3b opened (step 11) resumed each time at the recorded stage — staging intact and unapplied; replay from the cursor; ordinary intake closed until the replay report was complete; the scheduler started only after holds reloaded; no delivery before `delivery resume`.
- The evidence records show H1's restart prevention as attested by the owner and H2's as enforced by its volume marker, with the code that binds it to H2's entry; H2's restart attempt was refused.
- M was submitted once, by H1 at 10:15: its regenerated slots were held at creation, through a restart and `resume`, and closed only on the iPhone's report and the provider's Sent record, with writer unverified because no H1 journal linked the provider receipt (the scenario’s omniscient timeline names H1).
- **Attribution control, separate run.** Restore the same fixture with A, B and the same off-host prefix. This time retain H1’s actual stopped volume and carry a fresh challenge to it after stage 1 starts. Import its response binding the terminal sequence/hash and complete chain through M’s claim and exact provider reference. Use H3’s newly authorized scripted mailbox credential for evidence. Verify that the matching authenticated Sent record plus independently verified H1 journal/terminal supports attribution to H1. In the lost-tail run the identical Sent record can establish submission, but the header alone cannot establish H1 and no containment is triggered by it. In each run assert one Gmail submission for M, one APNs submission, no replayed side effect, and different attribution results based solely on the evidence visible to the restored Core. This fixture’s result does not change KF-1 or KF-2.
- H2's writes after its isolation reached E3 once: Z2 from the fenced tail; the derived writes derived again at 3b.
- No client accepted E2 or E3 before its confirmation, as a descendant or as a sibling.
- X, Y1, Y2, Z and Z2 are applied exactly once in E3; blob b is present once.
- The iPad's new key became known only through its verified bundle, and nothing in the bundle was trusted before verification.
- C is revoked; its post-revocation change is rejected.
- Every device's head is E3. The iPad's refreshed credential works, and its old bearer stays refused.
- P is never pushed, and the iPhone's local alert is the only one. Q is never resent.
- O stays held through regeneration, `resume` and a restart, is sent at most once more, only after the owner's release, and is recorded as delivered only on the owner's own finding.
- A discovery variant (S-10 step 2 (ii)) runs the same setup with the owner's inventory omitting H2: H3 promotes, the Mac reports H2's confirmed head when it comes back, and H3 is contained in that transaction; it then follows §8.5.1's sequence — H2 inventoried, fenced with its code, its tail staged, the inventory attested again, a supplementary confirmation superseding both heads, replay at 3a, ordinary work at 3b and delivery review at 4.
- **What N-A doesn't show:** a firing that H1 had moved earlier after the cut and sent, which E3 would evaluate again from A's schedule (KF-1, §17.1). N-A's M fires at its original due time; the moved-earlier case is S-11 (l), recorded against KF-1.

### 19.2 A rollback with acknowledgements, legacy requests settled before the R1 ledger, the installation of either v4 database, work Core committed but a device never learned, a later offline create and edit, overlapping exports, the original device lost, a crash during reconciliation, preference changes R1 can't guard, an idea created through R1's capture path, and the same work against the `prepare` backup installed twice (N-B)

**Setup.** Core is migrated from R1 and running with a Mac, an iPhone and an iPad. Core's export key was rotated earlier from Kx1 to Kx2. The owner keeps the documented key backup: current and retired export keys and the signing-key history. Shortly before the rollback, the iPad submitted operation 501, "create task T3 'Book the vet'". Core committed it, but the connection dropped before the receipt arrived. The iPad then went offline, still showing 501 as waiting, and changed T3's title (502). Two legacy requests, both submitted after `prepare`, are open: a preferences request from the frozen R1 CLI on the loopback owner listener, whose change 1 (an ordinary key) applied and whose change 2, an administrative key, is held (token `LR-q7`, generation 1) with changes 3–4 pending behind it; and a legacy device's receipts request that stopped after 2 of 5 receipts on a storage failure.

| # | Step | Evidence available | Allowed action | What the owner sees |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preflight | Device list, the counts each device last reported | `migrate rollback --preflight` creates generation R7 | "Rollback R7: waiting for acknowledgements from Mac, iPhone, iPad." |
| 2 | Acknowledgements | The iPhone syncs and signs `{R7, position 420, all outstanding counts 0}`. The Mac signs `{R7, position 310, quarantined 1}` and exports its quarantined R1 item, so its acknowledgement carries the export digest. The iPad is unreachable | Record acknowledgements; never infer the iPad's | "iPhone: acknowledged through 420 at <time>; nothing outstanding. Mac: acknowledged through 310; 1 quarantined item, exported (<digest>). iPad: no acknowledgement; last reported position 500 with 1 pending, at <time>." |
| 3 | Work after acknowledging | The iPhone, offline, creates task T2 "Call the plumber" (position 421) and edits its title (422). Core never learns of them | Saved locally; prompt to export | iPhone: "Saved on this device. Lisa Core is being rolled back; export this change." |
| 4 | Fence and settle | Acknowledgements; the iPad outstanding; the held preferences request and the partial receipts request | `--fence`: intake fenced; scheduler stopped; in-flight attempts settled; assignment cancels sent. The archive isn't written while either legacy request is open. The owner runs `--settle-legacy refuse-remaining`, which is killed after its first transaction and run again: `LR-q7` is refused once (the second run replays it), change 1 stays applied, changes 3–4 are `not_reached`, and R1's 403 is recorded; the receipts request is completed from its third receipt and its 204 recorded. Then the rollback archive is written with the Core-signed roster, the legacy parents and decisions, Core's final dispositions (501 among them, `accepted`), the projection map and both database identities | "Settled before rollback: 1 change waiting for review refused (1 earlier change kept, 2 later changes not applied); 1 receipt batch completed." Then "Fenced. Outstanding: iPad. Archive written: <path>, digest …" |
| 5 | Build, stage and verify | Archive, R1 directory, R1 executable, three manifest files | Generate I1, verify live fence under its lock, overlay the selected v4 database and seal the database and both sidecars. Run frozen R1 on a disposable copy of the exact candidate. Apply the per-candidate check to its real expected sets and both sidecars. Exercise frozen wire replay separately with this narrative’s seeded original bytes. Record the pass against the three hashes | The owner sees base and cut, verification result, acknowledged positions and unreported device work as unknown |
| 6 | Switch, interrupted | I1 and its verification receipt | Interrupt after one sidecar has been published. The live database still has the durable schema fence, so an attempted frozen start refuses. Under the same lock, resumption checks both sidecars and the pass, publishes the database last and records I1. Repeat after the final rename with a subsequent R1 write | The first interruption keeps R1 fenced. The second recognizes I1 and preserves its new write |
| 7 | R1 resumes | The verified installed directory | Start frozen R1. The frozen owner-scoped Mac queue resends the exact preferences request and records terminal 403. Its refused child and suffix do not execute. A separate authenticated receipts request replays 204 | The local harness reports the persisted queue status, the decoded owner-facing error and the receipt state. Evidence comes from the actual caller scope and frozen queue file |
| 8 | Two exports | The iPhone holds 421–422, then adds a note to T2 (423), sets an existing ordinary preference to a new value (424) and deletes another (425) | Protected export to Files (iCloud Drive), sealed to Kx2 and the iPhone's export key and signed by the iPhone; later a second export holding 421–425 | iPhone: "Exported 2 changes …", then "Exported 5 changes …" |
| 9 | iPhone lost | Only the two exported files remain | — | — |
| 10 | Inspect | The rollback archive, both iPhone archives, the key backup; R1's database, read-only | `lisa archive inspect` identifies the installed database as `reversed (R7)`, installation I1, from its native installation descriptor and protected receipt, checks that R1's listener serves this directory, and binds the session to all three. It deduplicates 421 and 422 across the two archives (same IDs and digests). Kx2 from the key backup decrypts them; the iPhone's signature, checked against the rollback archive's Core-signed roster through the key history, authenticates them; the one eligibility rule finds the iPhone active and unrevoked; the rollback-cut lookup finds 421–425 absent (Core never decided them). R1 has no task "Call the plumber" | "5 entries from iPhone (2 in both archives). Decrypted with export key Kx2; signed by iPhone and verified against the rollback roster. Entry 421: create task 'Call the plumber' — not known to Lisa Core before the rollback." |
| 11 | Reconcile 421; both processes killed | The owner approves 421 | The tool records the intent (request ID `lisa-rc-…`, body, "absent", time) and the `sending` mark, then sends `POST /v1/tasks`. R1 commits and is killed before writing its ledger record; the tool is killed before recording completion | Log: "421: sent; no answer." |
| 12 | Restart; the route's proof | An intent with a `sending` mark and no completion; a read-only snapshot of R1's database | The binding checks pass again (same installation and binding, current effects re-read and witnesses intact in this variant). No ledger record exists for `owner:lisa-rc-…`, so the create is indeterminate; no time window is used. The same snapshot holds exactly one task with 421's content, T-r1. The owner maps 421 to T-r1, and the mapping is recorded | "Entry 421 (create task 'Call the plumber'): R1 may have created it; R1's record of this request is gone or was never written. One task with exactly this content exists (task T-r1, created <time by R1's clock>). Use this task, send again (may duplicate), or skip." Then: "Mapped to T-r1." |
| 13 | Dependent edits | The mapping, revalidated by reading T-r1 | 422 (title) and 423 (note) go through `PATCH /v1/tasks/T-r1` with the revision read from R1, each with its intent recorded first | "Applied in R1: title updated (revision 2); note added (revision 3)." |
| 14 | The iPad is found | The iPad exports 501 and 502, and also an idea created offline (503) with a tag edit (504), sealed to Kx2. An older iPad archive was sealed with Kx1 before the export-key rotation | The rollback cut first: 501 is `accepted` with the same digest, so it becomes `applied_before_rollback` and is never sent; the projection map seeds T3's mapping to its R1 record, read back, and 502 goes to that record with its revision. 503 goes through `POST /v1/captures` with kind `idea`; its answer is lost on a dropped connection, and the safe resend returns the existing capture and idea; the mapping is recorded, and 504's tags go through `PATCH /v1/ideas/{id}`. The older archive opens with retired Kx1 from the backup; its draft waits for the owner | "iPad: 1 change was already in Lisa Core before the rollback ('Book the vet'); 3 entries applied; 1 draft needs your text confirmation." |
| 15 | A revoked device's archive | An archive from device C, revoked before the rollback, carrying an early creation time | The one eligibility rule makes it ineligible in `inspect` and `reconcile` alike; it is listed, and its content can only be re-entered deliberately | "Device C (revoked <time>): 1 entry not eligible. You can re-enter its content yourself." |
| 16 | Preferences R1 can't guard | 424 (a set of a present key) and 425 (a delete), with R1 running; the keys' records read from the selected database | Both are held: R1 checks `expectedRevision` only while a record exists and never compares record IDs, so a set sent through its API could recreate a deleted key or overwrite a replacement; R1 offers no conditional delete, and accepts preference deletes only as the owner's own change, which the owner confirms here. Later the owner quits R1 and runs `lisa archive reconcile --maintenance`: the tool takes R1's data-directory lock, finds 424's record with the same record ID, revision 3 and value, and sets it with R1's mutation and `preference.set` audit event in one transaction; finds 425's record unchanged and deletes it with R1's `data.deleted` audit event in another; R1 then starts normally | "424 and 425: Lisa R1 can't apply these changes safely while it runs. Quit Lisa R1 and run the maintenance session, or make them yourself in Lisa R1." After the session: "424 applied while Lisa R1 was stopped (record unchanged, now revision 4). 425 applied while Lisa R1 was stopped (record unchanged)." |
| 17 | Install the fallback twice on a disposable rehearsal | The original backup, final safety state, archives and current R1 installation | Install and verify I2 alongside its archived predecessor, using the same refusal and safety overlays. Change 1, absent from the backup, stays a candidate for maintenance. Send 501 once in I2. Before installing I3, detect and preserve both that create and a later independent R1 edit. Decline replacement once and prove the edit survives. In a second rehearsal deliberately select the inventoried replacement, verify its full preserved predecessor, publish I3, reject I2's session and resolve 501 before sending 502 with a fresh mapping | The inventory names the exact owner data that would be replaced. Safety protections survive either selection; no old mapping authorizes a write |
| 18 | Late export after R1 audit maintenance | The protected receipt and the installation and binding rows survive one-day audit pruning. A same-installation copy exists. | Run frozen maintenance, present the late export, stop R1 and run the locked rebind. Resolve only current proofs. Exercise a rolled-back copy, a copied binding, and a crash at each rebind boundary. | A genuine new action with established eligibility and approval can proceed. Each unproved earlier effect remains a named owner decision, and its dependants stay held. |

**Checks at the end:**
- No R1 ledger record was written while a legacy request was open; the held administrative change was refused once despite the interrupted settlement, change 1 stayed, changes 3–4 never applied, and the receipts were completed without repeating the first two.
- **On both databases,** the frozen queue's retry of the preferences request got R1's 403 from the installed ledger record and was marked failed; the refused change 2 and changes 3–4 never ran in R1; the receipts retry got 204.
- Require actual frozen starts at first install and reinstall to open a complete generation or refuse the live schema fence. Check both sidecars before the first runnable start. Account for each protected refusal, revocation, known delivery, budget charge and intervening owner edit.
- R1 holds T2 exactly once, with the later title and the note; rerunning `reconcile` repeats nothing.
- R1 holds T3 once — from Core's commit before the rollback — with the iPad's later title; its create was never sent again against the reversed database.
- Against the `prepare` backup, verify that T3 and change 1 follow the explicit decisions in steps 16–17. Check the preserved prior generation, inventory of intervening R1 work and mappings unique to the active installation.
- The entries present in both iPhone archives were reconciled once.
- R1 holds the iPad's idea once, created through R1's capture path, with its tags.
- No R2 scheduler ran after the fence.
- The rollback report named only acknowledged and reported positions; the iPhone's 421–425 appeared nowhere until its exports surfaced them; the iPad was never treated as clean, and the Mac's acknowledgement counted its quarantined item.
- Every executed entry was decrypted with an export key and authenticated by its device's signature against the rollback roster; one eligibility rule answered the same everywhere; C's entry stayed ineligible; drafts waited for the owner.
- The frozen R1 callers decoded every reply from the installed ledger, and nothing ran twice.
- `lisa archive log` shows each binding check, intent, the indeterminate create, the owner's mapping, the safe resend, the entry applied before the rollback and the sessions refused after a new installation.
- 424 and 425 were never sent through R1's API; each was applied only under R1's own lock, in one transaction that found its record unchanged.
- Verify recovery with a fresh archive-work directory, the rollback and device archives, the documented key backup, and the protected receipt installed on the actual R1 host. Missing past attempt or revocation evidence must surface as held work.

## 20. Traceability: the 15 original findings

"Preserved" marks the three findings whose revision-3 design the owner accepted (5, 6, 15). For the others, the third column names, first, the failure case the approval review of revision 8 found and the revision 9 rule that answers it, where there was one; then revision 8's, 7's and 6's, in the same way; then, in parentheses, the earlier rules still in force. Where an earlier entry differs from the operative sections, the operative sections govern. The front matter's first table maps revision 9's amendments to their deciding evidence. Evidence classes are those of §10.1.

Revision 6: Reverse migration wrote `{}` with the original success status, which the frozen typed capture and task clients can't decode; recorded errors echoed caller input (a category name, a weekday token, a preference key) → one table of decodable, content-free terminal replies for live, migrated and reversed records; error templates that name positions, never caller text. ## 22. Owner decisions, open decisions and unavailable evidence, by gate

**The owner's settled decisions, and the evidence that triggers each next step**

| Decision | Settled as | Evidence that triggers the next step | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| O-1–O-11, A-1, the permission scopes P-01–P-07 | As recorded in §15 and §16, unchanged: one Linux Core and Apple replicas; M0 first; route-by-route frozen-v1 compatibility; the digest-pinned `swift:6.4.2-trixie` build and slim runtime; locked dependencies; the original, unmodified R1 suite in debug and release, with any proposed R1 test change naming its file, reason, original outcome and evidence; `R2-CHECKPOINT.md` from M0; temporary R2 harnesses; the rootless engine (first use M2, first production image M4); amd64 and arm64 definitions kept, with R2 arm64 `NOT_RUN_ENVIRONMENT` until evidenced | Plan approval, then each milestone's evidence | G1–G5 |
| P-08 swift-certificates | Conditional: applies only if N-1 selects Design B after B's proof passes on every cell; then Claude brings the exact commit (candidate 1.21.1 at `84f03fafeca28c19f5fc4b528df3d22ce8896bd7`), the deciding proof, toolchain and lockfile compatibility, and the exact package and lockfile changes; the owner approves the exact pin before addition; proof-only provisioning stays separate. Recorded as inapplicable if Design A passes and is selected | N-1's recorded result at X1 | G3 (nearby) |
| **P-09 Proton Mail Bridge deployment** | **Decided by the owner on 2026-11-23: P-09a**, as revision 7 proposed it (§8.19.5): the official Bridge 3.27.2 for an amd64 Core; `protonmail-bridge_3.27.2-1_amd64.deb` (SHA-256 `cf08332d…a8dc`) and Proton's key (SHA-256 `d5279ed2…c221`); the sidecar and its deployment files; the Ubuntu base digest (and, in the same verification step, the policy file's digest) recorded at M7; account-free rootless development and testing once implementation is approved. **Not granted:** use of the owner's accounts, production deployment, host administration, image publication, later Bridge versions. P-09b wasn't chosen | Plan approval, then M7's account-free sidecar cases (Claude) and E-15 with the live Proton Mail cases (owner, LS-1 and LS-3) | G4 (Proton Mail) |
| Apple Developer account and APNs | The owner alone signs, provisions and runs the apps and live Apple checks; Claude is denied the account; the owner-run harness carries signing, registration, entitlements, profiles, APNs keys and token setup in its preflight, reads the signed products, and derives every identifier from the owner's own builds; credentials stay on the owner's systems; device availability is not verification | X1–X7 and LS-3, run by the owner | G3, G4 |
| Required integrations and account access | Mail and calendar on personal Outlook.com, Gmail with Google Calendar, iCloud and Proton; OpenAI, Anthropic and a self-hosted Ollama on Core and as the Apple fallback; Twilio Programmable Voice as in R1. Microsoft 365 tenants, Google Workspace and Ollama Cloud are outside the matrix. No owner account, credential, session or connected-account tool for Claude; the owner runs every credentialed and physical check; every calendar only with authenticated, private, read-only access | LS-1, LS-2, LS-3 and X5, run by the owner, after the prerequisites of §8.19 are verified | G4 |
| E-1 | A release requirement, applied at the initial URL and every redirect, IPv6 literals included. A failing platform keeps its gates open, and R2 isn't released there until a compliant redesign's proof passes there; deferral to Core is only a runtime fallback; only a redesign needing a new permission, dependency or requirement change comes back | X1, then X5 on each platform | G3, G5 |
| N-1 | A release requirement: nearby sync on Mac–iPhone, Mac–iPad and iPhone–iPad, each on shared Wi-Fi, with the Internet disconnected and with no access point. A design is adopted only after its own proof passes; if both fail, a redesign comes back as a plan revision, the gates stay open and the proofs repeat. X1 selects; X4 verifies production | X1, then X4 | G3, G5 |
| Calendar access policy | Authenticated, private, read-only for every required provider; no link anyone can open, even for a synthetic calendar | Each provider's LS-1 cases | G4 |

**No owner decision is pending.** P-09 was the last one, and it is decided. The proposed revision's corrections stay within the required behavior, the owner's settled policies and the existing permissions: fence codes, the staged replay, the stage split, `volume.json` and the `lisa-deploy` wrappers are Core and deployment mechanisms; the rollback installation and the maintenance session act on the owner's R1 data only under R1's own lock, started by the owner; the Bridge corrections are inside P-09a's accepted component; the harness's isolation check makes identity-only calls in the owner's own live runs; nothing adds a dependency or changes a lockfile.

| A genuine R1 Mac app for the upgrade rehearsal (E-12) | Unknown whether R1's Apple apps build — owner step at X3 | G3 (X7 part) |
| QR rendering in Core's CLI | Missing software: no approved QR encoder. Text pairing is complete, so nothing is blocked | None |

Claude's lack of access to the owner's accounts is not an item in this table: it is a standing boundary, and no case that needs an owner account is assigned to Claude. An authorable plan isn't a verified implementation: every gate above stays open until its evidence exists, and the two known findings stay visible until the release review.
